### Typed header access in production (M3-013)

Status: **production**, the M3-012 slice. The decision, rejected candidates, invariants, the accepted cost and the revisit conditions are "Typed header access decision (M3-012)" above; this section records what production does and how it differs from the slice as written.

- `src/muntin/headers_body.mojo` (new): public `WithHeaders[B: FromBody](Deinitable, Movable, _HeaderCarrier, _JsonBody where conforms_to(B, _JsonBody))` with `headers: Headers`, `body: B`, `__init__(out self, var body: B, var headers: Headers)` and `take_body(deinit self) -> B`; the private trait `_HeaderCarrier` with `_from_parts(body: String, var headers: Headers) raises -> Self` (`B.from_body`, a raise propagating). It imports `FromBody`, `Headers` and `_JsonBody` only. `muntin` exports `WithHeaders`.
- `src/muntin/app.mojo`: `_Route.headers: Bool` (default `False`). The eight body overloads pass `headers=conforms_to(B, _HeaderCarrier)`, and their last assert is `conforms_to(B, FromBody) or conforms_to(B, _HeaderCarrier)` with its message unchanged; no other guard changes. `App.handle` appends each request field's name and value for a carrier route only, after the body and the JSON verdict. The four body adapters assert `FromBody` or the carrier, and refine `B: FromBody` in the non-carrier branch before `B.from_body`. For a carrier they rebuild the fields (`_carrier_fields`, through `Headers.add`; a raise is `_internal_error()`), then build it with `B._from_parts` (a raise is `_bad_request()`). Differences from the slice as written:
  - the JSON steps are one helper, `_json_status[B]`, shared by the four adapters, with `_json_answer` for its 415/413. Non-carrier JSON bodies keep the exact arity (the verdict `"1"` and nothing after it); a carrier requires the verdict at its fixed index and an even count after it. The module comment, `App.handle`'s docstring, `_call_body`'s docstring and the first body overload's docstring describe the carrier.
- Unchanged: every overload signature, message and count (`get` and `post` keep ten each); `_Erased`, `_Call`, `_Bound`, `_handler_storage.mojo`, `check_unsafe.sh` and the unsafe surface; `State`; `http.mojo` (`Headers`, `Request`, `Response`); `body.mojo`; `json.mojo`; `state.mojo`; `testing.mojo`; `adapters/flare/muntin_flare.mojo`. `App` stays non-generic.
- Accepted cost, as decided: `WithHeaders` is a body-slot type but not a `FromBody`, so `FromBody` no longer names every type a `post` body slot accepts, and generic code bounded by `B: FromBody` does not accept the carrier. Building one by hand takes an explicit `Headers`.
- Behavior: on a carrier route the handler sees every field in order, with its casing, repeated names and empty values; Muntin chooses no status for the fields `input.headers` exposes and gives them no meaning. Two existing checks still answer before the handler: a `Json[T]` body's `Content-Type` verdict (415), and the rebuild of a field an in-memory `Headers` holds invalidly (the fixed 500). Order: 404 → query value 400 (`App.handle`) → route value 400 → for a `Json[T]` body 415 then 413 → field rebuild (the fixed 500, reachable only through the M3-002 `_fields` gap) → `from_body` 400 → handler → result conversion. Routes without a carrier receive the same arguments as before, and raw routes are unchanged.
- Diagnostics: the 262 must-fail fixtures common to `main` and the slice (`main`'s 267 less the five deleted carrier fixtures), built against each `src` with source paths and line numbers normalized: 251 identical (four of them differ before normalization only in the `muntin/__init__.mojo` import line the new export shifts), and 11 differ only in the source line the compiler echoes under the relaxed assert. They are the 11 the decision measured; their errors, messages, notes and carets are identical and every expected text matches.
- Evidence:
  - `tests/test_with_headers.mojo` (14), through `App.handle` and `TestClient` (`headers=`): one carrier handler on each of the eight body overloads, each sent fields, with fields in order, casing and repeats and the two transports equal; absent and empty fields; a handler-chosen 401 for a missing credential on the six JSON carrier routes; `from_body` 400 with fields present; the route value first (path and query); `State` with the reference count read inside handlers equal to the count between requests; `take_body` with a move-only body; `WithHeaders[Json[T]]` on all four body shapes (path and query route values) with the full order, each step measured with later failures present, including a field named `1` and two `Content-Type` fields; plain and JSON routes without a carrier, and raw `GET`, raw `POST` and stateful raw routes, unchanged with fields present; first registration wins in both orders; the `_fields` gap answered with the fixed 500 on every shape, after the route value, 415 and 413 and before the body; 404; a carrier built by hand; the raw-slot adapter function on production `App`, moved from the spike; and DX section 4's typed header access example.
  - `tests/with_headers_api_fail` (7, `check.sh`), on production `WithHeaders`: no `from_body` (`'WithHeaders[Note]' value has no attribute 'from_body'`), a nested carrier, `WithHeaders[Int]` and `WithHeaders[Json[T]]` without `FromJson` (`'WithHeaders' parameter 'B' has 'FromBody' type ...`), the body moved out (`field 'input.body.text' destroyed out of the middle of a value`), a carrier handler on `get` and a carrier before a route value on `post` (each a candidate note naming the carrier handler's own type). Each has one error. Built against `main`'s `src`, every one fails with `package 'muntin' does not contain 'WithHeaders'` instead, so each fails for its documented reason only with the slice.
  - `adapters/flare/test_localhost_roundtrip.mojo`: `json_app()` serves the stateful carrier route `POST /signed-greet` (`WithHeaders[Json[Greeting]]`, credential in the state). Over HTTP/1.1, repeated `X-A`/`x-a` fields reach the handler in order with their casing (200), a missing credential is the handler's 401, and a missing `Content-Type` is 415; each status and body equals `App.handle` with the test's fields. The handler echoes only `X-A` fields, because Flare's client adds fields of its own.
  - Mutations (planted in scratch copies of `src/muntin`, never in the tree), each red: the fields flag missing on each of the eight body overloads; the fields not appended; the fields appended for every body route (a plain `Json[T]` route answers 415); the carrier without `_JsonBody` forwarding; the first field read at the verdict's index; the JSON steps before the route value; a body conversion failure answered 500; the rebuild failure answered 400; the rebuild moved before the route value and the JSON steps (added after a fresh-context review found that order untested); the JSON flag missing with the parity check removed, both on every body overload and on carrier routes only; the carrier conforming to `FromBody` (`carrier_has_no_from_body` and `nested_carrier` build); the body bound relaxed with the check moved into `_from_parts` (`nested_carrier`, `carrier_of_int` and `carrier_of_json_without_from_json` build). Inert: the parity check removed alone. While every JSON carrier route sets its flag, no request tells it apart, and the JSON flag missing on carrier routes is red with or without it, as M3-009 recorded for its arity check.
- Spike retired: `tests/header_access_spike.mojo`, `tests/test_spike_header_access.mojo` and the five carrier fixtures in `tests/header_access_fail` are deleted, and `check.sh` registers `tests/with_headers_api_fail`. `tests/header_access_fail` keeps the three toolchain pins. The M3-012 record keeps what the spike measured.

### Registration structure decision (M3-014)

Status: **decision** (M3-014; `src/muntin` and `adapters/` are unchanged in it). It decides how registration grows past the ceiling M3-007, M3-008 and M3-012 measured. Today `get` and `post` have ten overloads each, Mojo 1.1.0's ten-note cap. Every typed shape costs four overloads: two result policies (`String`, `R: ToResponse`), each stateless and stateful. So typed header access on `get`, non-`Int` and several route values, `POST` without a body and broader raw handlers each push one method past the cap (`docs/SPEC.md`, "Remaining candidates").

**Selected: C4r, generic-arity slots on the existing method names. It reopens M2 when its production slice (M3-015) lands; this record is the explicit decision that M2-016 requires for that reopen.** Each registration method keeps one overload per *request-slot arity*, the number of request-derived handler parameters, in a stateless and a stateful family. The stateful family keeps a fixed leading `State[S]`, not counted in the arity; every request-derived parameter is a generic slot `var A`, and its kind is decided at compile time from its type. So slot arity 0 is `def()` and `def(State[S])`, slot arity 1 is `def(var A)` and `def(State[S], var A)`, and so on. Slot kinds:
- route value: `Int` today;
- body: a `FromBody`, or a `WithHeaders` carrier;
- raw: `Request`;
- `State`: first, through the stateful overloads.

The result type `R` is generic too, and its policy is chosen at compile time: `String` or `StaticString` give the 200 text response, a `ToResponse` converts itself. Which result types an overload accepts is a `where` clause checked by identity at the call site, because a check in the body cannot tell `StaticString` from other immutable-origin string slices. One compile-time rule check per overload gives each shape that selects an overload and passes its result `where` clause but breaks a registration rule a Muntin message; a rejected result type reports the clause's `violated constraint`. Call spellings, binding order, the request steps and their statuses, first-registration-wins and the storage do not change. What changes is in "C4r in detail" below: the overload declarations, the diagnostics of rejected calls, and three edges of the accepted set. C4r does not remove the ceiling. It turns the combinatorial growth along feature axes into linear growth in slot arity. It gives structural headroom through slot arity 4 (ten overloads per method). Full candidate-note visibility holds through slot arity 3 (eight overloads); at slot arity 4, a call that selects no overload can lose its last candidate note (a result rejected by the `where` clause adds an eleventh note), and that degradation is an accepted cost of this decision. Slot arity 5 (twelve overloads) is past the cap.

```mojo
# one overload per arity, stateless; the stateful family has the same arities after State[S]; every overload carries the same where clause
def get[E: Deinitable, R: Movable & Deinitable, //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def() thin raises E -> R) where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))
def get[A: Movable & Deinitable, E: Deinitable, R: Movable & Deinitable, //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(var A) thin raises E -> R) where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))
def get[A: ..., B: ..., E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(var A, var B) thin raises E -> R) where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))
def get[S: ..., A: ..., E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(State[S], var A) thin raises E -> R, state: State[S]) where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))
```

**Call syntax.** Every current route keeps its spelling under C4r, C1 and C4b. C2, C3 and C3v keep these spellings but add their own for new shapes (candidates below):

| Route kind | Spelling (today and under C4r) |
|---|---|
| stateless typed | `app.get["/users/{id}"](get_user)`, `app.post["/notes/{id}"](update)` |
| stateful typed | `app.get["/users/{id}"](get_user, users)` |
| raw, stateless and stateful | `app.get["/raw"](raw)`, `app.post["/raw"](raw, users)` |
| body | `app.post["/notes"](create)` with `def create(body: Note)` or `var body: Note` |
| `Json[T]` | `app.post["/users"](create_user)` with `def create_user(body: Json[CreateUser]) -> Json[User]` |
| `WithHeaders[B]` | `app.post["/users"](create_user, users)` with `input: WithHeaders[Json[CreateUser]]` |

**Measured** (Mojo 1.1.0 (8189361e)). Realistic candidates were built on scratch copies of the repository (git worktrees of `main`, not retained). The fixture comparison builds all 269 of `main`'s must-fail and route fixtures against each copy's `src` (every copy except C5, which does not compile), and `test.sh` runs every existing test against each, with paths and line:column normalized, and checks each expected text:

| Candidate | Overloads | Existing fixtures (269) | Existing tests (295) | Admits typed `get` headers |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1. status quo; a new method as its own set (`put`, ten overloads copied from `post`) | `get` 10, `post` 10, `put` 10 | identical | 295 pass; `PUT /notes/7` answers through the copy and `POST` to it is 404 | no: a `get` shape needs four more overloads (M3-012's table) |
| C2. a named surface per feature (`get_headers`, eight overloads: `def(Headers)`, `def(Int, Headers)`, stateful twins, both policies) | 10 + 10 + 8 | identical | 295 pass | yes, under a second name |
| C3. a builder holding the app (`app.at["/me"]().on["GET"](me)`, the engine's overloads on the builder) | builder 8 | identical | 295 pass | yes |
| C3v. a route value moved into the app (`app.add(get["/me"](me))`, free functions with the engine's overloads) | `add` 1, `get` 8 | identical | 295 pass | yes |
| C4b. the engine beside `get`/`post`, under a new name (`app.route["GET", "/me"](me)`) | 10 + 10 + 8 | identical | 295 pass | yes, under the new name |
| **C4r. the engine replacing `get`/`post` (selected)** | `get` 8, `post` 8 (arities 0 to 3) | 201 still match (134 identical); 68 lose their text; one of those compiles | **295 pass**; `check_flare.sh`, `check_unsafe.sh` and `check_boundaries.sh` pass | yes, under `get` |
| C5. the engine's adapters behind today's unchanged signatures | 10 + 10 | not reached | not reached | no |

The C4r scratch copy has arities 0 to 3, so eight overloads per method, and five `rebind_var` calls without the equality helper. The slice ships arities 0 to 2, so six overloads per method, with the helper and the guard's run-time `else`. Its fixture diff will differ in detail, and M3-015 measures it again.

Effect of each rejected candidate on what the acceptance names. "Same" means as production today:

| Candidate | `State`, ownership, move-only | raw `Request` | result and error policies | route literals, first-registration-wins | backend | `_Call`/`_Erased`/`_Bound`, unsafe |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | same | same | same | same | same | same |
| C2 | same; the new surface binds `State` as the stateful twins do | same | same | checked per surface; one route list | same | same |
| C3 | same, but writes through a stored `Pointer[App, mut]` escape interior-reference invalidation | same | same | same list; the builder registers late | same | same; a mutable borrow held across statements |
| C3v | same; the route value owns its box until `add` | same | same | checked in the free function; one route list | same | same |
| C4b | same | same | same | same; the new name is checked like C4r | same | same, plus the rebind helper |
| C5 | not reached (8 of 20 overloads do not compile) | | | | | |

- **C1, status quo.** The note budget is per method name. A failing call to the scratch `put` lists exactly `put`'s ten candidates, by the same rule `notes_are_per_method.mojo` pins below. So a new HTTP method fits the budget as its own copy, about 500 lines of overloads per method, and does not need restructuring; `docs/SPEC.md`'s grouping of methods with the others is corrected. Shapes on an existing method stay blocked: every `get` header shape drops candidate notes or reopens M2 (M3-012). A `String` route value costs `get` four more overloads (`def(String)`, both policies, each stateless and stateful) and `post` four, and `POST` without a body costs `post` eight. Deferring has a cost today. `/users/{name}` cannot be routed by a typed handler at all (M2-016 names non-`Int` route values as the most visible gap). Typed `get` handlers read headers only through application raw adapters (M3-012's `headers_of[me]`). DX section 3's `search(query: String, limit: Int)` has no typed form. **Rejected:** these stay blocked on Mojo 1.1.0 as long as each shape costs four overloads (the selection does not depend on the cap rising).
- **C2, named surfaces.** All 269 fixtures are identical. A header handler passed to `app.get` gets `get`'s ten notes and none names `get_headers`, so the right surface cannot be found from the error. A misuse on `get_headers` lists only that surface's candidates. Names multiply with combinations. Take headers or not × `Int` or `String` × zero to two route values: 7 route-value combinations × 2 = 14 shapes, at 4 overloads each that is 56 overloads, or 6 names of at most ten. **Rejected:** one concept spread over several names (M3-012 recorded the same for G), no error that leads to the sibling, and the name count grows with feature combinations.
- **C3, builder.** The chain registers and dispatches. A builder must hold `Pointer[App, mut origin]`, and the scratch copy reproduces M3-001's A2 gap with it: an interior reference into `App._routes` taken after the builder exists survives a hundred registrations through it and compiles (`registrar_blind_mutation.mojo` pins the mechanism). Splitting axes across builder types (`.state(users)`) still ends in one overload set for the shapes. **Rejected:** the blind-mutation path.
- **C3v, route value.** `app.add(get["/me"](me))` runs, and the route value owns its box, so no pointer is held. Shape selection is C4's engine. The only difference is the spelling, and every route changes to it: `app.get[route](h)` becomes `app.add(get[route](h))`, against DX's decision rule. **Rejected:** a spelling change for every route, with no capability C4r lacks.
- **C4b, engine beside.** All 269 fixtures are identical. Typed `get` headers run under `route["GET", ...]` (`def(Headers)`, `def(Int, Headers)`, `def(State[S], Headers)`). But `get` and `post` stay frozen, so `/users/{id}` stays `app.get[...]` while `/users/{name}` would need `app.route["GET", ...]`. That is two spellings for one method, permanently unless M2 is reopened later. **Rejected:** a second spelling for every in-method feature, against DX's decision rule and section 18. M2 stays closed only until one spelling is wanted.
- **C5, engine behind unchanged signatures.** Each of the 20 production overloads keeps its signature and names an engine adapter instead of its own. The twelve without an `Int` slot report no error. The eight with `def(Int)` or `def(State[S], Int)` fail: `cannot pass 'def(Int) raises E thin -> String' value, expected 'def(var Int) raises E thin -> String'` (the `_Bound` forms likewise). A typed borrowed value converts to no generic slot (`borrowed_overload_cannot_forward.mojo`, `typed_value_to_owned_slot.mojo`). So the engine and the signature change are one step and cannot be cut into an invisible refactor first. **Rejected** as a separate slice.
- **D. Generic descriptor (method and shape in one type or value).** One generic entrypoint over the handler's own type is not expressible: Mojo 1.1.0 has no variadic function types (`state_fail/variadic_handler_type.mojo`) and no reflection over function parameters. The method as a compile-time parameter (`route[method, path]`, measured as C4b) is expressible. A shape type the handler declares (a parts tuple `Parts[Int, Headers]` as its one parameter) is expressible with variadic struct parameters, but it replaces `def get_user(id: Int)` with positional access into a tuple. **Rejected:** the expressible forms are C4b's spelling or a handler-signature change.

**C4r in detail.**
- **Shapes and selection.** Arity selects the overload, and the argument count separates the stateful family, as today. Inside an arity no overload competes, so the raw `post` handler no longer depends on the "shorter parameter list" rule (`Request` is a slot kind). On the scratch copy, a call with more parameters than the largest arity gets `no matching method` with all eight candidates printed (9 notes, none omitted). Under the slice's six overloads, slot arity 3 costs two more per method (eight, every candidate note still printed) and slot arity 4 two more again (ten, where a rejected result can lose one candidate note: the accepted cost above).
- **What was measured.** The scratch engine classifies each slot with `comptime if A == Int` / `conforms_to(A, FromBody)`. It extracts with one helper per slot (`raises` a typed reject carrying the status) and keeps production's order, since route values come first and the body last: query 400 (`App.handle`), route value 400, 415, 413, rebuild 500, `from_body` 400, handler, result. It reuses `_Erased`, `_Call`, `_Bound`, `_handler_error`, `_json_status`, `_carrier_fields`, `_raw_request`, `_Route` and `App.handle` unchanged, and it kept the unused hand adapters, which older spikes import. All 295 existing tests pass on it, including the JSON and carrier order tests (`test_request_order_on_json_body_routes`, `test_json_inside_the_carrier_keeps_the_full_order`), and so does the Flare loopback suite. Typed `get` headers (a `Headers` slot) run on it with no overload added: `def(Headers)`, `def(Int, Headers)`, `def(State[S], Headers)`, and a bad route value is still 400.
- **The rule check must guard the adapter.** A first build reported an adapter's own empty `constraint failed:` for `def(String)` on `get`, not the rule. Mojo 1.1.0 elaborates the `_Erased.__init__[call=...]` statement's parameters before the rule function the overload called first. With the adapter named only under `comptime if <rule holds>`, every rejected shape reports its rule's message (`slot_misuse_names_the_rule.mojo` pins this on the spike). The Bool and the asserts must accept exactly the same shapes. Otherwise a stricter Bool would compile, register nothing and leave a silent 404. A compile-time `else: comptime assert False` does not close that hole well: Mojo 1.1.0 reports it instead of the rule (measured on the spike: `constraint failed: internal: guard and rules disagree` replaces the rule's message). So the guard's `else` is a run-time `abort` at registration. It keeps the rule's message, and a drifted Bool aborts the program at startup (the guard-drift mutation below).
- **Diagnostics of rejected calls.** All 269 fixtures still fail except one, `body_fail/post_owned_int_and_body` (below). 201 keep their expected text, 134 of them byte-identical. 68 lose it:

| Class change | M2 fixtures | M3 fixtures |
|---|---|---|
| `no matching method` + candidate notes → `constraint failed: <Muntin rule>` | 14 (`storage_fail` 6, `body_fail` 8) | 15 |
| `no matching method`, notes now naming the generic candidates (still rejected for the same reason, such as a `mut` or owned `State`) | 0 | 17 (`state_*_fail`) |
| constraint message reworded by the scratch engine's check order (same rule) | 11 (`compile_fail`) | 10 |
| now compiles (`var id: Int`) | 1 | 0 |

  Examples of the new messages: `a handler takes at most one Int route value` (`def add(a: Int, b: Int)` on `get`, which was ten notes ending in `cannot be converted ... to 'def(Int) raises Never thin -> String'`), `a raw handler returns Response`, `a GET handler takes no request body`, `the request body is the handler's last parameter` (the scratch copy also printed `the handler's result must be String or conform to ToResponse`, which the decided `where` clause replaces with `violated constraint`). The new primary line is `function instantiation failed` at the enclosing function, with the registration call in the next note and the rule last. That is the class production `post` already uses for a body type that is not a `FromBody` (`compile_fail/post_without_from_body`), and it is what 95 of `main`'s 269 fixtures (the route-literal checks among them) already show. The intermediate notes print internal parameters (`"k1": 1`), which the slice names or hides. The 21 reworded constraint messages are a scratch artifact: their rules are unchanged, so the slice keeps their texts. These classes were measured on the scratch copy, before the result `where` clause and with arities 0 to 3; they guide the slice, which re-measures and lists each old → new pair in the M3-015 production record. Two groups will differ in the slice: the six result-rule fixtures (`json_api_fail/result_without_to_json`, `json_fail/result_without_to_json`, `storage_fail/non_conforming_return_handler`, `storage_fail/error_type_is_not_a_result`, `body_fail/post_non_conforming_return`, `body_fail/post_int_body_non_conforming_return`) stay `no matching method`, now with `violated constraint`; and the arity-3 stateless shapes (`body_fail/post_int_and_two_bodies`, `state_post_fail/missing_state_argument_int_body`) select no overload under arities 0 to 2 and stay `no matching method`. The 68, by class on the scratch copy:
  - to `constraint failed`: `body_fail/get_body_handler`, `body_fail/post_body_then_int`, `body_fail/post_int_and_two_bodies`, `body_fail/post_int_body_non_conforming_return`, `body_fail/post_no_param`, `body_fail/post_non_conforming_return`, `body_fail/post_two_bodies`, `body_fail/raw_extra_parameter`, `json_api_fail/result_without_to_json`, `json_fail/result_without_to_json`, `state_get_fail/missing_state_argument`, `state_post_fail/missing_state_argument`, `state_post_fail/missing_state_argument_int_body`, `state_post_fail/state_without_body`, `state_raw_fail/get_extra_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/get_int_before_request`, `state_raw_fail/get_missing_state_argument`, `state_raw_fail/get_string_return`, `state_raw_fail/get_typed_return`, `state_raw_fail/post_extra_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/post_missing_state_argument`, `storage_fail/error_type_is_not_a_result`, `storage_fail/non_conforming_return_handler`, `storage_fail/raw_get_string_return`, `storage_fail/request_param_handler`, `storage_fail/string_param_handler`, `storage_fail/two_param_handler`, `with_headers_api_fail/carrier_before_route_value`, `with_headers_api_fail/carrier_on_get`;
  - still `no matching method`, new candidate notes: `state_get_fail/state_last`, `state_post_fail/mut_state_parameter`, `state_post_fail/owned_state_parameter`, `state_post_fail/plain_value_parameter`, `state_post_fail/state_after_body`, `state_post_fail/state_after_route_value`, `state_post_fail/state_for_stateless_handler`, `state_raw_fail/get_mut_state_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/get_owned_state_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/get_plain_value_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/get_state_after_request`, `state_raw_fail/get_state_for_stateless_raw_handler`, `state_raw_fail/post_mut_state_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/post_owned_state_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/post_plain_value_parameter`, `state_raw_fail/post_state_after_request`, `state_raw_fail/post_state_for_stateless_raw_handler`;
  - reworded constraint: `compile_fail/post_int_body`, `compile_fail/post_int_body_without_from_body`, `compile_fail/post_int_int`, `compile_fail/post_request_param`, `compile_fail/post_string_body`, `compile_fail/post_without_from_body`, `compile_fail/raw_post_typed_return`, `compile_fail/state_post_int_as_body`, `compile_fail/state_post_typed_int_body_without_from_body`, `compile_fail/state_post_without_from_body`, `compile_fail/typed_post_int_body`, `compile_fail/typed_post_int_body_without_from_body`, `compile_fail/typed_post_int_int`, `compile_fail/typed_post_without_from_body`, `json_api_fail/body_without_from_json`, `json_api_fail/from_json_alone_is_not_a_body`, `json_fail/body_without_from_json`, `json_fail/from_json_alone_is_not_a_body`, `state_raw_fail/post_int_before_request`, `state_raw_fail/post_string_return`, `state_raw_fail/post_typed_return`;
  - now compiles: `body_fail/post_owned_int_and_body`.
- **Three changes to the accepted set** (an M2 reopen, besides the declarations). For plain `def` handlers the result side matches production: every result type production's `String` overloads accept (`String`, `StaticString`, and a handler declared `-> StringLiteral[...]`, whose function type returns `String`) registers on the model with the same answer, and the types production rejects are rejected (with the result rule below):
  - *widened:* an owned route value, `def h(var id: Int, body: Note)`, registers. Slots are `var A`, because owned bodies (`var body: Note`, move-only bodies) must keep registering. Borrowed slots reject them (the borrowed-slots mutation below). M2-009's fixture `body_fail/post_owned_int_and_body` pins the old rejection. The handler receives its own copy of an `Int`, so nothing else observes the difference.
  - *narrowed:* an explicitly typed function value, or a helper parameter, whose type has a borrowed `Int` route value no longer registers: `def(Int) ...`, `def(Int, var B) ...`, `def(State[S], Int) ...` and `def(State[S], Int, var B) ...`, all with `thin raises Never` or a generic `E`. On `main` these register. A borrowed body or `Request` in a typed value already fails on `main` (`def(Note) thin raises Never -> String` on `App.post` is the same `TODO` error, and DX already requires `var Request`), so those positions do not change. The leading `State[S]` is not a slot: `def(State[S], var Request) thin raises Never -> Response` and `def(State[S]) thin raises Never -> String` still register. Examples: `var f: def(Int) thin raises Never -> String = get_user; app.get["/u/{id}"](f)`, or a generic `def register[E](mut app: App, h: def(Int) thin raises E -> String)` forwarding to `app.get`. It fails with `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet`. The `var` spelling (`def(var Int) thin raises Never -> String`) registers on C4r. `main` rejects that spelling (`no matching method`), so code written for one does not compile on the other. Plain `def` handlers, the form every DX example uses, are unaffected, and typed values with no parameter or only owned ones (`def() ...`, `def(var Request) ...`) still register. No existing test registers a typed borrowed value, which is why all 295 pass. The slice adds the spelling rule to DX next to the existing `raises Never` rule.
  - *widened:* an explicitly typed function value or helper parameter whose result type is `StaticString` (`var f: def() thin raises Never -> StaticString`, or a generic `def register[E](mut app: App, h: def() thin raises E -> StaticString)` forwarding to `app.get`) registers as text. With a borrowed `Int` route value (`def(Int) thin raises Never -> StaticString`) it is the `TODO` error on both, under the narrowed edge; the `var Int` spelling registers on the model and is `no matching method` on `main`. On `main` it is the `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet` error, because production's text overloads take `def(...) -> String` and a typed value does not convert. On the model `R` is exactly `StaticString` and passes the `where` clause. Both the local and the generic helper are measured: spike test `test_typed_static_string_value_registers` registers each, and on `main` each is the `TODO` error. A handler value typed `-> StringLiteral[...]` is the `TODO` error on both, so it does not change.
- **The result rule is a `where` clause.** A text-result check written in the overload's body (`R == String or R == StaticString`) would widen the accepted set: Mojo 1.1.0's generic `==` keeps an origin's mutability but not its identity (`registration_known_gaps/generic_equality_ignores_origin_identity.mojo`), so every immutable-origin `StringSlice` result a handler can express and bind would pass as `StaticString`. Production accepts only `StaticString` itself. Measured with the body-level check on the spike model, the forms that bind and would register are:
  - `StringSlice[ImmutAnyOrigin]` (also spelled `ImmUnsafeAnyOrigin`, `AnyOrigin[mut=False]`, `ImmOrigin(MutAnyOrigin)`). Safe code can give it data, including a slice of a dropped local (`registration_known_gaps/safe_dangling_any_origin_slice.mojo`: the explicit `StringSlice[ImmutAnyOrigin](local)` constructor erases the origin), so copying it would read freed memory;
  - `StringSlice[ImmUntrackedOrigin]` (only the empty value can be built safely);
  - a local's origin through an explicitly instantiated parametric handler (`def origin_text[o: ImmOrigin]() -> StringSlice[o]`, registered as `origin_text[origin_of(local)]`), one type per local, empty value only. Even a direct comparison reports this type equal to `StaticString`;
  - the `_`-prefixed interior and subtree origins of the static origin (undocumented members, evidence only), in handlers that always raise.

  Mutable origins stay unequal and are rejected. A result origin tied to a handler argument does not bind to the generic overload. The raw MLIR spelling `#lit.static.origin` crashes the compiler on registration in production and in the model. The list is what was measured, not a complete enumeration.

  No check inside a generic body tells these apart. `==` against `StaticString` or `StringSlice[ImmStaticOrigin]`, a wrapping struct or tuple, a function type, a struct over the origin parameter, `reflect[R].name()` (it prints the origin erased) and `conforms_to` all erase the origin. `Origin.equals` may be used only in `where` clauses (`registration_fail/origin_equality_not_in_comptime_if.mojo`). An exact `-> StaticString` overload beside the generic one makes a `String` handler fail on the exact candidate (`registration_fail/exact_static_overload_rejects_string.mojo`).

  A `where` clause on the overload is checked at the call site, by identity: `where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))` is printed as `identical(R, StringSpan[ImmStaticOrigin])` and so on. With it on all six spike overloads, every form above is rejected (`registration_fail/immutable_origin_result_rejected.mojo`), while `String`, `StaticString`, `Response`, `Json[T]` and other `ToResponse` results keep registering (all spike tests pass). So M3-014 closes the result edge with this clause: the result types accepted for plain `def` handlers equal production's (typed `-> StaticString` values are the third edge above).

  The cost: a rejected result type no longer gets the Muntin rule message. A `-> Int` handler gets `no matching method` with `candidate not viable: violated constraint` and the expanded `identical(...)` condition, which is production's class for result errors today (`no matching method` with `does not conform to trait 'ToResponse'`). A clause that calls a function to keep the message (`where not _loose_static[R]() or R == StaticString`) is `lacking evidence to prove correctness` even for `String`, and a composite predicate measured in review let immutable-origin slices through, so the clause is pinned exactly as written. A result with a local's immutable origin (`origin_text[ImmOrigin(origin_of(s))]`) is rejected differently, as `invalid call to 'on': lacking evidence to prove correctness`; it is still rejected.
- **`rebind_var`.** `comptime if A == Int` does not refine `A` (`header_access_fail/generic_slot_does_not_refine.mojo`), so a parsed `Int` (and a `String` result) reaches the handler through `rebind_var`. On Mojo 1.1.0 `rebind_var` accepts a different struct with the same layout (`registration_known_gaps/rebind_var_layout_twins.mojo`: `Meters` read as `Seconds`) and rejects a different layout (`registration_fail/rebind_var_layout_mismatch.mojo`). So every rebind, of slots and of the result alike, sits behind a type-equality assert in one helper (`_as[T, A]` in the spike; `guarded_rebind_rejects_layout_twin.mojo`). For origin-free types (`Int`, `String`, `Request`, `Headers`) that equality is exact, and the rebind is an identity cast. For `StringSlice` the helper's own assert is not exact (generic `==` drops the origin), so exactness for results comes from the `where` clause: a text result reaching the helper is `String` or exactly `StaticString`, and the rebind is an identity cast. The helper's message says what it checks itself: `rebind requires generic type equality`. `check_unsafe.sh` does not flag `rebind_var` today. Adding `rebind` to its existing pattern would exempt the storage module, so the slice adds a dedicated check: exactly one `rebind_var[` in all of `src/muntin`, storage module included, comments and docstrings counted; it is in `app.mojo`, on the line after `comptime assert A == T`; and the `rebind_var` import appears only in `app.mojo`.
- **Unchanged under C4r:** `State` and its guarantee (the handle is still copied once into `_Bound` and borrowed per request); move-only bodies and handlers; the raw `Request` escape hatch (still the whole request, `Response` only, no route values); `ToErrorResponse` and the fixed 500; `Json[T]` and `WithHeaders[B]` as bodies; route-literal validation at the registration call, with the placeholder count matched against the route-value slots; first-registration-wins and the 404/400/415/413/500 boundaries (`App.handle` is unchanged); backend independence (no adapter change, `check_flare.sh` passes); `_Call`, `_Erased`, `_Bound` and `_handler_storage.mojo`. `App` stays non-generic.
- **Implementation and storage cost.** The ten hand adapters (`_call_none` to `_call_state_raw`) become one adapter per arity, stateless and stateful, over a shared slot extractor and result policy. The scratch file grew by 300 lines (to 1,942) while it still contained the unused hand adapters. Nothing is allocated per request beyond today's raw arguments; registration still boxes one value per route. The `rebind_var` helper and its `check_unsafe.sh` rule are new. Build time was not compared (the timing runs were cache-warm).

**How C4r admits the remaining candidates.** Each of these is its own later item, and the counts are per method:

| Candidate | Cost under C4r | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| typed `get` headers | one slot kind (`Headers`) or the `WithHeaders` carrier; no overload up to arity 2, two per method once a handler takes three slots, such as `def(Int, Int, Headers)` (spike and scratch) | A `Headers` slot beside a non-carrier `Json[T]` body needs the carrier's even-count arity rule, because the exact arity answers 415 when fields follow the verdict. |
| `String` route values | one slot kind and converter, no overload (spike) | Raw `String` is a route value and never a body (decided below). |
| several route values, path and query together | rule changes; no overload up to arity 2, then two per method for slot arity 3 and two more for slot arity 4 (ten; full candidate notes only through slot arity 3) | Binding stays positional, in the literal's order. |
| more HTTP methods | `put`, `patch` and `delete` each get the same arity overloads (six after M3-015), delegating to the shared engine | The method's own rules (body or not) live in the check. |
| `POST` without a body, raw route values, `ToResponse` raw results | rule changes, no overload | |
| another injected kind | still a separate argument, so it doubles the stateful family as today | Unchanged by C4r; M3-001's condition applies. |

**`String` is a route value, never a body.** Binding by type works only for disjoint types (M2-005), so raw `String` cannot be both a route value and a body. This holds under any positional design, not only C4r: once the status quo adds `String` route values, `def(String)` on `post` is just as ambiguous. M3-014 decides it: raw `String`, like `Int`, is a route-value type, as DX section 3 writes `search(query: String, ...)` and as SPEC's `/users/{name}` candidate needs. A body is never a raw `String`. A text body is a Muntin type conforming to `FromBody`, and its name and shape are decided by the body item that adds it. Whether other builtins (bytes, numbers) can be bodies is not decided here; that is the body item's decision. M3-015 changes nothing here: `String` stays no slot kind there, and `def(String)` on `post` keeps today's message. The String route-value item makes `String` a value kind.

**Why C4r rather than deferral or C4b.** The in-method features (`String` and several route values, typed `get` headers, `POST` without a body) are blocked on Mojo 1.1.0 under every candidate except a restructure: C1 measures them past the cap. Of the candidates that unblock them, only C4r keeps one spelling per method. C4b avoids the M2 reopen only while `get` and `post` stay frozen beside a second entrypoint. C2 hides the right surface from the error. C3 adds a write path the compiler does not track. C4r's costs are the diagnostics of rejected calls (of the 68 texts lost on the scratch copy, 29 changed class, 17 kept `no matching method` with new candidate notes, 21 were rewordings the slice avoids, and 1 compiled; in the slice the six result-rule and two arity-3 fixtures among the 29 stay `no matching method`), two widened edges and one narrowed edge, the `rebind_var` discipline, and result errors reported by the `where` clause instead of a Muntin message. In exchange, rejected calls that select an overload name the Muntin rule they break, as DX section 16 asks, instead of up to ten candidate notes. Calls that select an overload report one rule, not candidate notes. Calls that select none list every candidate, which fits the cap at six or eight overloads; at ten, a result rejected by the `where` clause adds a `constraint declared here` note that pushes out the last candidate, and some stateful calls that select no overload add two trailing notes after the candidates (observed for `var db: State[Db], id: Int`, `mut db: State[Db], id: Int`, `db: Db, id: Int` and `id: Int, db: State[Db]`: 8 notes for 6 overloads; `var db: State[Db]` alone adds none), which drop after the candidates. Each in-method feature then becomes a slot kind or a rule, not four overloads. One cost goes the other way: the primary error line moves from the registration call to the enclosing function, and the call appears in the next note. M3-015 adds no new feature family, but it intentionally widens two accepted edges (`var Int` route values, typed `-> StaticString` function values) and narrows one (typed function values with a borrowed `Int` route value). Its payoff comes in the later items. The restructure still goes first and alone, so that the re-pinned diagnostics, the three edges and the reopened contract are reviewed in one PR without a new feature family; otherwise they would be mixed into the first feature's tests. The reopen is decided here, at this record's merge. Undoing it needs a new decision, not a review of M3-015; M3-015's own review checks that the slice matches this record.

**Invariants** (for M3-015 and after):
- One overload per arity and per family (stateless, stateful) on each method, at most ten per method. A result rejected by the `where` clause adds one note for its candidate, so at ten overloads (slot arity 4) such an error prints eleven notes and loses one: the accepted cost. The argument count separates the families, and no two overloads of a method accept the same handler.
- A slot's kind is decided from its type alone; positional binding: route values in the literal's order, the body last, `State` first.
- Every rejected handler shape that selects an overload fails with a Muntin message, through a rule check that guards the adapter; the primary error line is the enclosing function and the registration call is in the next note. Shapes that select none (a wrong `State` convention, more parameters than the largest arity, a result type outside the `where` clause) keep `no matching method` with the candidate notes. A stateful handler registered without its state selects the stateless overload of its arity, when that arity exists, and gets the `State` rule's message. A result with a local's immutable origin is rejected as `invalid call ... lacking evidence to prove correctness` rather than `no matching method`, and a typed function value with a borrowed `Int` route value fails with the `TODO: function type conversions` error before any overload is chosen. Rules that existed before keep their message.
- The guard's Bool and the rule asserts accept exactly the same shapes; the guard's `else` aborts at registration.
- Every overload carries the same result `where` clause, so the result types accepted for plain `def` handlers equal production's and a text result reaching the rebind helper is `String` or exactly `StaticString`.
- Every `rebind_var` goes through one helper that asserts type equality (exact for origin-free types; for results, exactness comes from the `where` clause), and `check_unsafe.sh` allows it nowhere else.
- Raw `String` is never a body; once `String` becomes a slot kind it is a route value.
- The request steps, their order and statuses, first-registration-wins, `App.handle`, `_Route`, `_Call`, `_Erased`, `_Bound` and the storage module are unchanged by the restructure.
- The raw `Request` handler stays the escape hatch on every method, stateless and stateful.

**Migration and compatibility.** Every plain-`def` registration keeps compiling and behaving as before: all 295 tests pass on the scratch engine (which kept the hand adapters the older spikes import). Application code changes only where it spells a typed function value or helper parameter with a borrowed `Int` route value; it then adds `var` to that parameter (`def(var Int) ...`, `def(State[S], var Int) ...`) and keeps a leading `State[S]` as it is. Borrowed bodies and `Request`s in typed values already need `var` today. The compiler rejects the borrowed spelling with `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet`, which names no fix, so DX states the rule. Code that relies on `var id: Int` being rejected has nothing to migrate. The M2 completion contract is amended in M3-015: overload declarations, the diagnostics of rejected calls, and the three edges above. Spellings, binding, results, errors, the raw escape hatch, ownership and the backend seam are unchanged.

**Evidence:**

The model: one overload per arity, 0 to 2, stateless and stateful, every registration boxed, with each slot classified at compile time (`Int`, `String`, body, carrier, `Request`, `Headers`, `State`). The rule check is both asserts and a Bool that guards the adapter, and the guard's `else` aborts at registration. Every overload carries the result `where` clause. The result policy is chosen at compile time. The one rebind helper asserts type equality (exact for origin-free types). Production's `_Erased`, `_Bound` and `_handler_error` are used unchanged. ### Registration structure amendment: generic forwarding (M3-015)

Status: **decision amendment** to "Registration structure decision (M3-014)", made in M3-015 after the slice was implemented. M3-014 decided three edges of the accepted set. The slice accepts more than that, and M3-014's revisit condition "M3-015 cannot keep ... the accepted set beyond the three recorded edges: re-decide before merging it" fired. This record is that re-decision. M3-014's three edges stand as written: edge 2 is still the typed `-> StaticString` function value and helper parameter.

**Found:** a helper that forwards a handler to `get` or `post` can now be generic in two ways that the per-shape overloads did not accept:
- over the handler's result, with its own `where` clause proving the registration's;
- over a request parameter, with the slot's kind decided when the helper is instantiated.

M3-014 measured the typed `StaticString` value and helper (edge 2) and the typed `def(var Int)` spelling, but no helper generic over these types. External review of PR #44 flagged that recording this under edge 2 widened M3-014's meaning without a decision.

**Edge 4, generic forwarding (accepted).** An explicitly typed function value or helper parameter whose result or request-parameter types are generic rather than the concrete types the per-shape overloads required registers when the rules hold for the instantiated types and the compiler can prove the registration's `where` clause from the helper's own constraints at the forwarding call. That clause is not checked against the instantiated type alone: a helper whose generic result has no `where`, or a partial disjunction of the clause's branches (measured: `String or StaticString`, `String or conforms_to(R, ToResponse)`, `StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse)`, `conforms_to(R, ToResponse) or String`), is rejected at the forwarding call with `invalid call to 'get': lacking evidence to prove correctness`, even when instantiated with an accepted type. One branch (`R == String`, `R == StaticString`, `conforms_to(R, ToResponse)`) or the whole clause, also with its branches reordered, proves it. Measured with Mojo 1.1.0 (8189361e) against `main` (the merged M3-014, per-shape overloads) and the slice, each form forwarding to `App.get` or `App.post`:

| Form (helper parameter) | `main` | slice | Edge |
|---|---|---|---|
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, `where R == String` | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, `where R == StaticString` | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> String`, `get` `/x/{id}`, `A = Int` (owned or borrowed handler) | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(State[S], var A) thin raises Never -> String`, stateful `get` | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(var A, var B) thin raises Never -> String`, `post` `/x/{id}`, `Int` then a body | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> Response`, `A = Request`, on `get` | `no matching method` | registers (raw) | 4 |
| `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> Response`, `A = Request`, on `post` | `constraint failed: Request is the whole request, not a body; ...` | registers (raw) | 4 |
| `h: def(State[S], var A, var B) thin raises Never -> String`, stateful `post`, `Int` then a body | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(State[S], var A) thin raises Never -> Response`, `A = Request`, stateful raw on `get` | `no matching method` | registers (raw) | 4 |
| the same on `post` | `constraint failed: Request is the whole request, not a body; a stateful raw handler ...` | registers (raw) | 4 |
| `h: def(var Note) thin raises Never -> R`, `where R == String`, on `post` | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> R`, `where R == String`, `get` `/x/{id}` | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, `where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))` (the registration's clause; reordered branches register too) | `no matching method` | registers | 4 |
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, no `where` | `no matching method` | `invalid call to 'get': lacking evidence to prove correctness` | none (rejected on both) |
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, `where (R == String or R == StaticString)`, given a `StaticString` handler | `no matching method` | `invalid call to 'get': lacking evidence to prove correctness` | none (rejected on both) |
| `h: def() thin raises Never -> R`, `where conforms_to(R, ToResponse)` | registers | registers | none |
| `[R: ToResponse] h: def() thin raises Never -> R` | registers | registers | none |
| typed `def() thin raises Never -> Response` value | registers | registers | none |
| `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> String` on `post`, a body (unbounded `A`) | registers | registers | none |
| `[B: FromBody] h: def(var B) thin raises Never -> String` on `post` | registers | registers | none |
| typed `-> StaticString` value; helper `h: def() thin raises E -> StaticString` | `TODO` conversion error | registers | 2 (M3-014) |

The first rows come from the slice's own probes; the stateful post, stateful raw, generic-`R`-on-`post`, slot-and-result, verbatim-clause, no-`where` and disjunction rows were found by the fresh-context review of this amendment and re-measured. The table lists what was probed, not every possible form; edge 4 is the class above. Each form that registers answers as the plain handler would: same status, body, request steps and rules. The rules still reject what they rejected for plain handlers. For example, a generic `A` that becomes `Float64` is `constraint failed: a get handler's parameter ...`.

**Decision:** edge 4 is accepted, and C4r is kept. It follows from the selected structure: the overloads take generic slots and a generic result, so generic code that instantiates them with accepted types reaches them as a plain handler does. Alternatives weighed:
- not merging (D0), keeping the per-shape overloads: this undoes M3-014's decision, which needs a new decision, and edge 4 adds no request behavior or unsoundness that a plain handler does not already have;
- narrowing edge 4 inside C4r: the overloads see only the instantiated types, and no other way to tell a helper's forwarding from a direct registration was found. None was measured, so this is a judgment and not a proven impossibility.

Nothing that registered on `main` stops registering because of edge 4; two forms rejected on both trees change their message (the `lacking evidence` rows). The M2 completion contract is amended for four edges (`docs/SPEC.md`).

**Evidence:** `tests/test_registration.mojo` pins each `main`-differing row, plus the conformance branch as a control: `test_generic_result_helper_forwards_a_text_value`, `test_generic_result_helpers_forward_each_where_branch`, `test_generic_slot_helpers_forward` and `test_more_generic_forwarding_forms` (which also registers the whole clause with its branches reordered). `tests/registration_api_fail/generic_result_disjunction_lacks_evidence.mojo` and `generic_result_without_where_lacks_evidence.mojo` pin the failing side of the boundary, a partial disjunction and no `where` (each `no matching method` built against `main`). The probes for every row above were built against both trees (scratch, not retained).

**Revisit when:** a toolchain change lets an overload see how a caller spelled a type (reconsider whether edge 4 should stay open), or a later item adds a slot kind (generic forwarding then reaches it too, and that item states it; M3-016 states it for `Headers`).

### Registration on generic-arity slots in production (M3-015)

Status: **production**, the M3-014 slice with one decision amendment. The decision, rejected candidates, invariants and revisit conditions are "Registration structure decision (M3-014)" above, and the fourth accepted-set edge is "Registration structure amendment: generic forwarding (M3-015)"; this section records what production does and the measured diagnostics. It reopens M2 as that record decided (the overload declarations, the diagnostics of rejected calls and three edges of the accepted set) and as the amendment added (a fourth edge).

- **Final overloads** (`src/muntin/app.mojo`), six per method, every one carrying `where (R == String or R == StaticString or conforms_to(R, ToResponse))` exactly:

  ```mojo
  def get[E: Deinitable, R: Movable & Deinitable, //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def() thin raises E -> R)
  def get[A: Movable & Deinitable, E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(var A) thin raises E -> R)
  def get[A: ..., B: ..., E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(var A, var B) thin raises E -> R)
  def get[S: Movable & Deinitable, E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(State[S]) thin raises E -> R, state: State[S])
  def get[S: ..., A: ..., E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(State[S], var A) thin raises E -> R, state: State[S])
  def get[S: ..., A: ..., B: ..., E: ..., R: ..., //, path: StaticString](mut self, handler: def(State[S], var A, var B) thin raises E -> R, state: State[S])
  # post: the same six signatures
  ```

  Each body is `_check[method, stateful, path, R, A, B]()` (absent slots are the private `_NoSlot`), then `comptime if _admits[...]():` around the one `self._routes.append(_route[Last](method, path, _Erased.__init__[call=<adapter>](...)))`, else `abort("registration rule check and guard disagree")`. `_route[Last]` sets `_Route.body`, `json`, `headers` and `raw` from the last slot's type.
- **Slot kinds** (`_kind[A]`): `_NoSlot` absent; `A == Int` a route value; `A == Request` raw; `conforms_to(A, _InjectedState)` a misplaced `State`; `FromBody` or `_HeaderCarrier` a body; anything else no kind (`String` and `Headers` included). Type equality is exact for these origin-free types.
- **Rules** are one ordered function per method, `_get_rule` and `_post_rule`, over the slot kinds, whether `R == Response`, and the literal's path and query placeholder counts; `_rule` adds the malformed-literal check first. It returns `_OK` or one code per message. `_check` asserts `rule != <code>` once per message, and `_admits` is `_rule(...) == _OK`. The order keeps every message the per-shape overloads gave for the same shape: `post`'s one-slot path checks the placeholders, then `Int`, then `State`, then `FromBody`, as the old body overload did, and a `Request` with a result other than `Response` is also reported after the placeholders (the old body overload's order; a raw handler returning `Response` gets the old raw overload's placeholder messages); its two-slot path reports a first slot other than `Int` before the placeholders (only shapes the old overloads did not select get there), then checks the placeholders and the body slot in the old order. Messages kept (every constraint text an existing fixture expects): `malformed route literal`, the two `route declares ... but the handler takes none`, `handler takes one Int parameter; ...`, `handler takes only the request body; ...`, `handler takes one Int parameter and the request body; ...`, `Int is a route-value type, ...`, the two `Request is the whole request, not a body; ...`, `State is injected application state, not the request body; ...`, `a handler takes at most one State, as its first parameter`, and the two `... request body; its type must conform to FromBody`. New messages, each naming the method: `a get handler takes no request body`; `a get handler takes at most one Int route value`; `a get handler's parameter is an Int route value or, for a raw handler, the Request`; `a raw get handler takes only the Request and returns Response`; `a stateful raw get handler takes State first, then only the Request, and returns Response`; `State is injected application state; a stateful get handler takes State first, and the state is the registration's second argument`; `a post handler takes the request body as its last parameter`; `a post handler takes one request body, as its last parameter`; `a post handler's parameter before the body is an Int route value`; `a raw post handler takes only the Request and returns Response`; `a stateful raw post handler takes State first, then only the Request, and returns Response`. On `get`, a second `State` reuses `post`'s existing `a handler takes at most one State, as its first parameter`. A last assert, `internal: a registration rule has no message`, fires only if a code lacks its assert.
- **Adapters and request order:** `_call_0`, `_call_1`, `_call_2` and `_call_state_0` to `_call_state_2` replace the ten hand adapters. Each converts its slots in order with `_slot[A, at]` (`raises _Reject`, a status), calls the handler alone in its `try` (`_handler_error[E]`), then `_respond[R]`. A slot's raw index is its position, because the only route value precedes the body. `_slot`: `Int` through `_parse_int` (400); `Request` through `_raw_request` (500 on failure); a body: for `_JsonBody`, `_json_status` first (415, 413), then for a carrier `_carrier_fields` (500) and `_from_parts` (400), else `from_body` (400). `_rejected` maps 400 to `_bad_request`, 500 to `_internal_error` and 415/413 through the kept `_json_answer`. `_respond`: `String` and `StaticString` are `Response.text`, anything else (`Response` included, unchanged by its own `to_response`) is `to_response()`. The order is production's: 404, query 400 (`App.handle`), route value 400, 415, 413, field rebuild 500, `from_body` 400, handler, result. `_Respond`, `_text` and `_converted` are gone; `_Route`, `App.handle`, `_json_status`, `_carrier_fields`, `_raw_request`, `_handler_error`, `_Bound`, `_Call`, `_Erased` and `_handler_storage.mojo` are unchanged except docstrings (`_Route.raw`, `App.handle`).
- **The one rebind:** `_as[T: Movable, A: Movable](var value: T) -> A` asserts `A == T` (`rebind requires generic type equality`) and returns `rebind_var[A](value^)` on the next line. `_slot` uses it for `Int` and `Request`, `_respond` for `String` and `StaticString`. `scripts/check_unsafe.sh` now also requires exactly one `rebind_var[` in `src/muntin` (storage module, comments and docstrings included), in `app.mojo` on the line after `comptime assert A == T`; fails if any other module names `rebind_var` or `std.builtin.rebind`; and requires `app.mojo`'s import to be exactly `from std.builtin.rebind import rebind_var`. The check covers `rebind_var` as decided; the prelude's reference form `rebind`, which needs no import, is not used in `src/muntin` and is not checked (a possible follow-up).
- **The accepted-set edges, as shipped** (`tests/test_registration.mojo`, 10 tests):
  - widened: an owned route value registers, `def(var id: Int)` on `get` (path and query) and `def(var id: Int, body: Note)` on `post`, stateless and stateful, with the route value still converted before the body;
  - widened (edge 2, as M3-014 decided): a typed `var f: def() thin raises Never -> StaticString` value and a generic helper parameter `h: def() thin raises E -> StaticString` forwarded to `app.get` (each the `TODO` error on `main`) register and answer 200 with the text. A plain `-> StaticString` handler and a `-> type_of("literal")` handler answer as a `String` one;
  - widened (edge 4, by the amendment above): helpers generic over the result or over request parameters, as enumerated there; the measured `main`-differing forms are pinned by the generic-forwarding tests;
  - narrowed: `var f: def(Int) thin raises Never -> String = by_id; app.get["/users/{id}"](f)` fails with `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet` (`tests/registration_api_fail/typed_borrowed_int_value.mojo`), and `def(var Int) thin raises Never -> String` registers and answers (`test_typed_var_int_value_registers`).

  Pre-change checks against `main` (`src` of the merged M3-014): the borrowed typed value built; the `def(var Int)` spelling and the plain `def(var id: Int)` handler were `no matching method in call to 'get'`; the typed `StaticString` value and the generic helper were each the `TODO` error; the `-> type_of("lit")` handler registered and answered `lit`.
- **Result errors.** A result outside the `where` clause is `no matching method` with the candidate's `violated constraint` note when the compiler can decide the clause at the call. When it cannot, the error is `invalid call to '<method>': lacking evidence to prove correctness`. This happens for a result with a local's immutable origin (`origin_text[ImmOrigin(origin_of(s))]`, `tests/registration_api_fail/local_origin_result_rejected.mojo`; on `main`, `no matching method`, the result failing the `ToResponse` bound; M3-014 measured it on its model, and the slice re-measured it in production). It also happens for a forwarding helper's generic result that its own constraints do not pin down (the amendment above; `generic_result_disjunction_lacks_evidence.mojo`, `generic_result_without_where_lacks_evidence.mojo`). The local-origin fixture's text names the error class; the recorded mutation (the clause removed, after which it compiles) ties it to the clause.
- **Diagnostics.** All 278 must-fail and route fixtures of `main` were built against `main`'s `src` and the slice's, with absolute paths, `line:column` and the import line numbers in `Included from` normalized, and compared in full:
  - 141 identical;
  - 77 keep their primary error and `constraint failed:` text; their chain gains the `_check` frames (`call expansion failed with parameter value(s): (..., "stateful": False, ...)`, then `function instantiation failed` at `_check`), and the caret shows the `_check` assert. This includes every one of the 21 texts the M3-014 scratch copy had reworded;
  - 14 keep `no matching method` and their pinned note, with six candidate notes instead of ten (eleven `state_*` fixtures and `storage_fail/linear_error_type`), or a changed adapter name in the storage fixture's own code (`storage_fail/downstream_move_header`, `storage_fail/mismatched_trampoline`, below);
  - 45 are re-pinned, listed below with each old and new expected text, and `body_fail/post_owned_int_and_body` compiles (the widened edge) and is deleted.

  `storage_fail/mismatched_trampoline` pairs `hello` with the production arity-1 adapter `_call_1[Int, Never, String]` and is re-pinned from `... to 'def(Int) raises Never thin -> String'` to `... to 'def(var Int) raises Never thin -> String'`; `storage_fail/copy_erased` and `downstream_move_header` use a local trampoline and keep their texts. The scratch classes of the M3-014 record held: the six result-rule fixtures and the arity-3 `body_fail/post_int_and_two_bodies` stay `no matching method`, while `state_post_fail/missing_state_argument_int_body` (also arity 3) keeps its `missing required argument: 'state'` note unchanged. No fixture changed its error class except as listed.

  result rule: `no matching method` with the trait note → `no matching method` with the `where` clause's `violated constraint` (6):

- **Kept message pinned:** `compile_fail/raw_post_typed_return_placeholder.mojo` (`def h(req: Request) -> String` on `post["/x/{id}"]`) expects `handler takes only the request body; route must declare no path or query parameter`, as on `main`. The first rule order reported the `Request` rule there; no fixture covered the shape, and the fresh-context review found it.
- **New production fixtures** (`tests/registration_api_fail`, 18, `check.sh`): `typed_borrowed_int_value.mojo` (the narrowed edge); `local_origin_result_rejected.mojo`, `generic_result_disjunction_lacks_evidence.mojo` and `generic_result_without_where_lacks_evidence.mojo` (above); `slot_misuse_names_the_rule.mojo` (a `Float64` parameter reports the rule, the guard's purpose); `rebind_rejects_layout_twin.mojo` (`_as[Meters, Seconds]` is `constraint failed: rebind requires generic type equality`); and `immutable_origin_result_{get,post}_{,state_}{0,1,2}.mojo`, one per method, family and slot arity, each registering a `StringSlice[ImmutAnyOrigin]` result through that overload and expecting `identical(R, StringSpan[ImmStaticOrigin])`. The `get` arity-2 and `post` arity-0 fixtures (and their stateful twins) use a shape the overload rejects for any result: two `Int` values and no body.
- **Spikes and toolchain pins.** Deleted: `tests/registration_spike.mojo`, `tests/test_spike_registration.mojo`, and `registration_fail/{slot_misuse_names_the_rule,guarded_rebind_rejects_layout_twin,immutable_origin_result_rejected}.mojo`, replaced by the production fixtures above. Kept: the six other `tests/registration_fail` pins and the three `tests/registration_known_gaps` pins, with their comments pointing at production; `raw_fail/equal_parameter_lists.mojo` stays as M2-014's toolchain pin, though production no longer relies on the rule. `state_spike`, `raw_spike`, `error_spike` and `error_response_spike` hold local copies of what they imported (`_Respond`, `_text`, `_converted`, and in the first two the hand adapters, `_call_raw` in `state_spike`), with header comments saying they are production's as of M3-014; `scoped_state_spike` imports them from `state_spike`. Their tests, fixtures, known gaps and `lib_only` drivers keep every expected text.
- **Tests:** `test.sh` runs 30 files and 305 tests (main: 30 files, 302 tests; the 7 spike tests left with their file and 10 production tests came with `tests/test_registration.mojo`, so 305 on the slice); every existing production test passes unchanged in its assertions. `check_flare.sh` passes (the Flare loopback suite registers through the new overloads).
- **CI:** PR #44, `verify` and `flare` on ubuntu-latest and macos-latest, before and after the amendment and on its final HEAD. A review scoped to the fixes confirmed them with probes against `main` (the restored message on stateless and stateful handlers, 34 related fixtures, and further generic-result helpers). Re-measured for the amendment: the `where R == StaticString` helper registers only on the slice, while the `where conforms_to(R, ToResponse)` helper registers on `main` too.

### Typed get header access decision (M3-016)

Status: **decision** (M3-016; `src/muntin` and `adapters/` are unchanged in it). It decides how a typed `get` handler reads request header fields without taking the whole raw `Request`. M3-012 left this out because every measured `get` shape exceeded the ten-note cap or changed an M2 signature; M3-014 made it a slot kind or the carrier on the arity overloads, and M3-015 shipped those overloads. The measurements start from M3-015's production (`get`/`post` with six overloads each, `_get_rule`/`_post_rule`, the guard, the one rebind helper).

**Selected: a `Headers` request slot on `get`, as the handler's last request parameter.** A `Headers` slot is the request's whole ordered header collection with M3-002's semantics, rebuilt per request as a fresh, Muntin-owned value. It adds no overload, no public type and no injected kind.

```mojo
def me(headers: Headers) raises Unauthorized -> String:
    var token = headers.get("authorization")         # Optional[String]
    if not token:
        raise Unauthorized()                         # ToErrorResponse: 401
    return "me " + token.value()

def note(id: Int, headers: Headers) -> String: ...                   # route value, then the fields
def me_in(users: State[Users], headers: Headers) -> String: ...      # State first, as today
def note_in(users: State[Users], id: Int, headers: Headers) -> String: ...

app.get["/me"](me)                       # slot arity 1
app.get["/notes/{id}"](note)             # slot arity 2
app.get["/me"](me_in, users)             # stateful slot arity 1
app.get["/notes/{id}"](note_in, users)   # stateful slot arity 2: State + Int + Headers fits the six overloads
```

- **Shapes on `get` after the slice:** `def()`, `def(Int)`, `def(Headers)`, `def(Int, Headers)` and the raw `def(Request) -> Response`, each also after a leading `State[S]`. The `Int` comes from a `{name}` path segment or a `{key}` query item, as today. Results and errors are unchanged: `String`, `StaticString` or a `ToResponse`, through the same `where` clause, and the declared error type's `ToErrorResponse` or the fixed 500.
- **The kind** is decided by exact type equality, `A == Headers`, beside `Int` and `Request` (all origin-free, so the equality and the rebind through `_as[Headers, A]` are exact). It is not a trait: no application type can opt in, so this adds no request-injection mechanism. A request slot is still an `Int` route value, a body, a `Request` or now `Headers`; `State` stays the registration-bound injected parameter.
- **Position: last, once.** A `Headers` slot follows at most one `Int` route value and is the last request parameter, as a body is on `post`. Two reasons, both measured. The transport needs no new rule: `_route[Last]` reads the route's flags from the last slot, and a slot's raw index is its position, so a last `Headers` slot sets the existing `_Route.headers` flag, and `App.handle`, unchanged, already appends each field's name and value after the route value on a route without a body. And the story stays one: on both methods the fields sit in the last request position, as a `Headers` on `get` and inside the body carrier on `post`. On the spike, accepting `def(Headers, Int)` by relaxing the rule alone compiles and crashes on the first request (`index 1 is out of bounds`): the flag comes from the `Int`, so no field is transported and the `Int` is read past the end. Position independence would need an index rule in `_slot` and a flag rule in `_route`, for a second spelling of the same handler; M3-014's spike measured such an index rule (`Headers` declared before a route value, and its mutation "the `Headers` index ignoring the route values"), so it is expressible, and the reason to reject it is the second spelling and the extra rules, not feasibility.
- **Ownership:** the adapter rebuilds one `Headers` from the transported strings through `Headers.add` and moves it into the handler, which may declare `headers: Headers` (borrowed) or `var headers: Headers` (owned); a `mut headers: Headers` parameter selects no overload (`no matching method`, as on production today). The handler's value is its own: changing it does not change the `Request` that `App.handle` received (measured). It is not a view of `request.headers`, because `_Call` carries only the raw argument strings (M3-002's R1 transport); lending the request's own `Headers` needs `_Call` to carry the `Request`, which is M3-012's existing revisit condition for the per-request copy. No backend buffer or lifetime reaches the value.
- **Fields and missing fields:** exactly M3-002's `Headers` (the same type `Request.headers` and `WithHeaders[B].headers` hold): every field in order, original casing, repeated names as separate fields, empty values as values, ASCII case-insensitive `get` (first value or `None`) and `get_all`. Muntin chooses no status for a field and gives it no meaning; a missing field is `None`, and its status is the handler's error type to choose (M3-012's rule, unchanged).
- **Request order on a `Headers` route:** 404 → query value 400 (`App.handle`) → route value 400 → field rebuild → handler → result. The rebuild fails only for a field written through the M3-002 `_fields` gap, and the answer is the fixed 500, as for carrier routes and raw routes; a bad route value is still 400 with such a field present. No new 400, 413 or 415 path. Routes without a `Headers` slot receive the same raw arguments as before.
- **Cost per request,** on `Headers` routes only: each field's name and value is copied twice (into the raw arguments, then into the rebuilt `Headers`, whose `add` validates it again), as carrier and raw routes already pay.
- **Raw `Request`:** unchanged and still the escape hatch. A handler cannot take both: `def(Request, Headers)` and `def(Headers, Request)` keep the raw rule's message.
- **`WithHeaders[B]` and `post`:** `post`'s last request position is its body, so typed `post` handlers keep reading fields through the carrier (`input.headers`), and `post` takes no `Headers` slot. Every `Headers` shape on `post` keeps today's message (measured byte-identical, below): `def(Headers)` is `the handler's parameter is the request body; its type must conform to FromBody`, `def(Headers, B)` is `a post handler's parameter before the body is an Int route value`, `def(B, Headers)` is `a post handler takes one request body, as its last parameter`, and `def(Int, Headers)` is `the handler's last parameter is the request body; its type must conform to FromBody`. A `Headers` slot beside a body would be a second spelling of the carrier, and `State + Int + Headers + B` would be slot arity 3 (eight overloads per method).
- **Typed function values and generic forwarding:** a typed value spells the slot `var Headers` (`var f: def(var Headers) thin raises Never -> String` registers; `def(Headers) thin raises Never -> String` is `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet`, on production today too, the same toolchain cause as for `Int`). M3-015's edge 4 reaches the new kind, as its amendment's revisit condition said a new kind would: `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> String`, `def(var A, var B)` (an `Int` then `Headers`, so `B = Headers`) and `def(State[S], var A)` forward to `get` with the `Headers` slot generic and answer as the plain handler (measured on the spike).
- **M2 stays closed.** No signature, binding rule, result policy or status boundary changes: the slice adds accepted `get` shapes, as M3-013 added the carrier to `post`, and rewords one M3-015 rule message (below), which keeps the contract's statement that a rejected call that selects an overload reports the Muntin rule it breaks.

**Rules and diagnostics** (the slice's rule edits, measured on a scratch copy of production):
- `_kind`: `A == Headers` is a new kind, checked after `Request`.
- `_get_rule`: one branch after the existing `State`, `Request`, body and no-kind checks, so their messages still win, and before the two-`Int` check: a first slot `Headers` must be alone (then the placeholder rules of a handler without a route value), and a second slot `Headers` follows an `Int` (then the one-route-value placeholder rule). Anything else with a `Headers` slot is the new rule `a get handler takes one Headers, as its last parameter`.
- `_post_rule`: a first slot `Headers` before another slot is reported as a first slot of no kind (`k1 == _OTHER or k1 == _HEADERS`); every other `post` path already reports `Headers` as it reports a type of no kind. Without that edit `Headers` would take the route-value branch: on a route without a placeholder `def(Headers, B)` changes to `handler takes one Int parameter and the request body; ...`, and on a route with one placeholder it registers and binds the route value as a field name and the body as its value (`post["/x/{id}"]` answers 200 with the field `7=hi`; mutation, below).
- The no-kind message on `get` names the new kind: `a get handler's parameter is an Int route value or, for a raw handler, the Request` becomes `a get handler's parameter is an Int route value, the request Headers or, for a raw handler, the Request`. The change is necessary: the message lists what a `get` parameter may be, and keeping it would make it false. It is the only existing compiler text that changes; it re-pins `storage_fail/string_param_handler` and `registration_api_fail/slot_misuse_names_the_rule`, and `docs/DX.md` section 3 quotes it.

Measured with Mojo 1.1.0 (8189361e), each shape built against M3-015's `src` and against the scratch copy (selected design):

| Shape (on `get` unless named) | M3-015 | Selected |
|---|---|---|
| `def(headers: Headers)`, `def(var headers: Headers)` on `/x` | `constraint failed: a get handler's parameter is an Int route value or, for a raw handler, the Request` | registers |
| `def(id: Int, headers: Headers)` on `/x/{id}` and `/x?{id}` | the same | registers |
| `def(State[Db], Headers)`, `def(State[Db], Int, Headers)` with the state | the same | registers |
| typed `def(var Headers) thin raises Never -> String` value | the same | registers |
| `def(Headers, Int)`, `def(Headers, Headers)`, `def(State[Db], Headers, Int)`, `def(State[Db], Headers, Headers)` | the same | `constraint failed: a get handler takes one Headers, as its last parameter` |
| `def(Headers)` on `/x/{id}`; on `/x?{k}` | the same | `route declares a path parameter but the handler takes none`; `route declares a query parameter but the handler takes none` |
| `def(Int, Headers)` on `/x` and on `/x/{a}/{b}` | the same | `handler takes one Int parameter; route must declare exactly one path or query parameter` |
| `def(Headers, String)` on `/x/{s}` | the same | the reworded no-kind message |
