| `def(Request, Headers) -> Response`, `def(Headers, Request) -> Response` | `a raw get handler takes only the Request and returns Response` | identical |
| `def(Headers, Note)`, `def(Note, Headers)`, `def(Headers, WithHeaders[Note])` | `a get handler takes no request body` | identical |
| `def(Headers, State[Db])` without the state; `def(State[Db], Headers)` without the state | `State is injected application state; a stateful get handler takes State first, ...` | identical |
| `def(Headers, State[Db])` with the state; `def(mut headers: Headers)`; `def(Int, Int, Headers)`; `def(State[Db], Int, Int, Headers)`; `def(Headers) -> Int` | `no matching method in call to 'get'` (six candidate notes; the result's `violated constraint`) | identical |
| typed `def(Headers) thin raises Never -> String` value | `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet` | identical |
| `def(Headers)` on `get["x"]` | `malformed route literal` | identical |
| 16 `post` shapes with `Headers` (alone; before or after a body; after a route value; with and without placeholders; stateful; beside a `Request`; two `Headers`; beside a carrier; `def(Int, Headers, Note)`; `-> Int`) | today's messages (above), `no matching method` at slot arity 3 and for the result | identical |

"Identical" means the whole normalized compiler output is byte-identical, not only the message. The table lists what was probed (46 shapes), not every possible one.

**Candidates** (Mojo 1.1.0 (8189361e); scratch copies are git worktrees of M3-015's HEAD, not retained):

| Candidate | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1. `Headers` slot on `get`, last, once | on a scratch copy, `check.sh` (all 308 fixtures, with the two re-pins), `test.sh` (the 305 existing tests unchanged, plus 11 scratch tests) and `check_flare.sh` (with a stateful `GET /signed/{id}` loopback route: 200, the handler's 401 and a route-value 400, each equal to `App.handle`) exit 0; `check_unsafe.sh` passes with no new `rebind_var`; diagnostics above; no overload, no `App.handle` or `_Route` change | **chosen** |
| 1′. the same slot in any position | relaxing the rule alone compiles and crashes on the first request (above); it needs index and flag rules for a second spelling of one handler | rejected |
| 2. a Muntin wrapper (`RequestHeaders`, field `headers: Headers`) as the slot type | on its own scratch copy: the same 11 tests pass (ported to `h.headers`), the 308 fixtures give the same result as candidate 1 (two message-only differences, the message naming the wrapper), and `check_unsafe.sh` and `check_flare.sh` (the loopback route ported to the wrapper: 200, 401, 400) pass. Ownership, transport, order, composition and forwarding are candidate 1's. It adds a public type and a `.headers` hop, and under it `def(headers: Headers)` stays rejected, although `Headers` is the type `Request.headers`, the carrier's field and M3-012's raw-slot adapter (`f: def(Headers)`) already use. The wrapper's internals could change later, but so can `Headers`'s, which is also Muntin-owned | rejected (no material gain) |
| 3. a `FromHeaders` conversion trait as the slot kind (`from_headers(headers: Headers) raises -> Self`, a raise answered before the handler) | expressible on Mojo 1.1.0 (scratch copy): an application `ApiKey(FromHeaders)` registers on `get`, `Headers` itself can conform (a non-raising `from_headers`), and candidate 1's tests pass unchanged on it, as do its 308-fixture comparison (the same two message-only differences), `check_unsafe.sh` and `check_flare.sh` (the same loopback route). Transport, rebuild, storage and the one rebind are candidate 1's; the conversion call is the only addition. Typed values and generic forwarding of an application type (`ApiKey`): a typed `def(var ApiKey) thin raises Never -> String` value registers, and the borrowed `def(ApiKey)` spelling is the `TODO` conversion error, as for every slot; a generic helper `h: def(var A) thin raises Never -> String` forwards an `ApiKey` handler to `get` and it registers both with `A: FromHeaders` and with no bound beyond `Movable & Deinitable`. Each answers 200 with the field and Muntin's 400 without it. So once a trait is a slot kind, generic code can reach a conforming application type through M3-015's edge 4 without a bound naming the trait. Its costs: Muntin must choose the status of a raise (the copy answers 400; M3-012 rejected a Muntin status for header values, since the right one differs per field); a type that conforms to both `FromBody` and `FromHeaders` is classified by `_kind`'s order without a word (on `post` it is rejected with `its type must conform to FromBody`, which it does; on `get` it registers as headers); several header conversions in one handler (`def(ApiKey, Trace)`) are rejected by the one-slot rule, and allowing them is more slot arity. A trait opt-in is a typed request-injection mechanism, which this item does not decide | rejected now; additive later, because `Headers` can conform to such a trait without changing the shapes chosen here |
| 4. a get-side header carrier around a route value (`WithHeaders`-like, no body) | not built: it is candidate 2 with the route value inside it, and `WithHeaders[B]` requires `B: FromBody`, which an `Int` is not (M3-012's `WithHeaders[Int]` fixture) | rejected |
| D0. defer: typed `get` handlers stay without header access | no new shapes, nothing to migrate if a later request-scoped context covers headers. Cost today: a `get` route that needs one field is raw (no typed route value, raw literals declare no placeholder, no result policy) or uses an application raw-slot adapter (`headers_of[me]`, M3-012), which also has no route value | rejected: the selected slot adds no type, overload or status, changes no existing behavior outside the one reworded message, and is the shape M3-014 named |

**Why the slot.** It delivers the type every other header surface already uses (`Request.headers`, `WithHeaders[B].headers`, `TestClient`'s `headers=`), with the same semantics, transport, rebuild and 500, as a fresh value the handler owns or borrows. It adds no public type, no status for a field and no opt-in mechanism, and it leaves every other route as it is: `App.handle`, `_Route`'s fields, the storage, the result clause and every `post` message are unchanged, and of the existing diagnostics only the message that lists `get`'s parameter kinds changes. That it also fits the arity overloads with no new overload is what M3-014 predicted, measured here; it is not the reason for the choice. The wrapper adds a type without a gain, the trait adds a status and a precedence problem this item has no reason to take on, and deferring keeps typed `get` routes that need a field raw.

**Invariants:**
- `Headers` is a request slot kind by exact type equality only; no trait and no application type is a header slot. `State` remains the only injected kind.
- On `get`, a `Headers` slot is the last request parameter, once, after at most one `Int` route value; `post` takes no `Headers` slot (its fields come through `WithHeaders[B]`), and every `post` message for a `Headers` shape is the one it had before.
- The handler's `Headers` is a fresh value rebuilt from the transported fields, with M3-002's semantics; Muntin chooses no status for a field. The only pre-handler failure it adds is the fixed 500 of the rebuild (the `_fields` gap).
- No overload is added: `get` and `post` keep six each. `App.handle`'s code, `_Route`'s fields, `_Call`, `_Erased`, `_Bound`, the storage module, the result `where` clause and the unsafe surface (one `rebind_var`) are unchanged.
- Typed routes without a `Headers` slot or a carrier receive no header strings; raw routes are unchanged.

**Evidence:**
- `tests/get_headers_spike.mojo` and `tests/test_spike_get_headers.mojo` (11 tests). The spike models production `get` with the `Headers` kind: its six `get` overloads are free functions with production's signatures and `where` clause; it imports production's `_kind`, placeholder counts, rule codes, `_Erased`, `_Bound`, the arity-0 adapters, `_slot`, `_as`, `_rejected`, `_respond` and `_handler_error`, registers on a production `App` and dispatches through production `App.handle`, unchanged. It copies `_get_rule` with the `Headers` branch, `get`'s messages and the four slot adapters with a `Headers` branch in the slot. Tests: fields in order, casing, repeats and empty values; an owned slot is a fresh value (the request's fields unchanged); a missing field answered by the handler's error type (401); the route value first, path and query, with 400s; `ToResponse`, `StaticString` and raising handlers; `State` with the reference count unchanged across requests; the `_fields` gap answered with the fixed 500, after a route value's 400, and never on a route without the slot; routes registered through production `get` and `post` (an `Int` route, a raw route, a carrier route) unchanged with fields present; `TestClient` (`headers=`); first registration wins in both orders; a typed `def(var Headers)` value and generic forwarding (`def(var A)`, `def(var A, var B)`, `def(State[S], var A)`, and a typed helper parameter).
- `tests/get_headers_fail` (9, `check.sh`), each with one error:
  - on the spike: `headers_before_route_value` and `two_headers` (`constraint failed: a get handler takes one Headers, as its last parameter`; against production `get` they fail with the no-kind message instead, so each fails for its documented reason only with the kind); `headers_then_body` (`a get handler takes no request body`: the `Headers` rule runs after the body rule; on production the same text, since there `Headers` is no kind); `typed_borrowed_headers_value` (the `TODO` conversion error; the same on production, a toolchain premise; its inverse is the spike test's `def(var Headers)` value);
  - on production `post`, the messages the slice must keep: `post_headers_alone`, `post_headers_before_body`, `post_headers_before_body_with_route_value` (on `/x/{id}`, the case the `_post_rule` edit exists for), `post_body_then_headers`, `post_route_value_then_headers` (texts above). They pass on production today by design.
- Scratch copies (not retained): candidate 1 with the measurements above; its diagnostics: all 308 fixtures built against M3-015's `src` and the copy's, with absolute paths, `line:column` and import line numbers normalized: 306 byte-identical, and the two re-pinned ones differ only in the message line. The 46 shape probes in the table, built against both. Candidates 2 and 3 on their own copies, with the measurements in the candidates table (for candidate 3 also four probes of an application `ApiKey`: a typed `var` value, the borrowed spelling, and generic forwarding with and without a `FromHeaders` bound).
- Mutations of the spike (planted in scratch copies, never in the tree), each red:
  - `Headers` classified by production `_kind` (no kind): the spike test stops building, and `headers_before_route_value` and `two_headers` lose their text;
  - the `headers` flag not set for a `Headers` slot: ten of the eleven spike tests fail (the handler sees no field);
  - the rebuild failure answered 400: the `_fields` gap test fails;
  - `def(Headers, Int)` accepted: `headers_before_route_value` compiles;
  - two `Headers` accepted: `two_headers` compiles;
  - the rebuild read from index 0 instead of the slot's position: six tests fail;
  - a condition added to the guard only (rejecting `Headers`): the spike test aborts at registration with `registration rule check and guard disagree`;
  - the `Headers` rule moved before the `State`, `Request` and body rules: `headers_then_body` loses its text.
- Mutations of the scratch copy of production, each red: without the `_post_rule` edit, `post_headers_before_body` loses its text (and three `post` probes change to `handler takes one Int parameter and the request body; ...`), and `post_headers_before_body_with_route_value` compiles (the route value bound as a field name, above); `Headers` accepted as a `post` body, `post_headers_alone` and `post_route_value_then_headers` compile; the `Headers` branch first in `_get_rule`, four `get` probes change to the new rule's message (`def(Headers, Request)`, `def(Headers, Note)`, `def(Headers, WithHeaders[Note])`, `def(Headers, String)`).
- Review: a fresh-context review re-ran the fixtures, probes and mutations. It found that the `_post_rule` edit's case on a route with a placeholder was unpinned (now `post_headers_before_body_with_route_value`), that the slice's docs list missed DX section 3, README and the injected-slot bullet, and that the rejected candidates lacked their fixture, unsafe and Flare measurements (now measured, above). A review scoped to those fixes confirmed them. After the rebase onto `main` with M3-015 merged, a fresh-context review of the final HEAD found one stale sentence (fixed) and nothing else open.
- CI: PR #45, `verify` and `flare` on ubuntu-latest and macos-latest.

**Cost.** One existing message changes (the `get` no-kind message, two re-pinned fixtures). `_Route.headers` comes to mean "the route's handler receives the request's fields" (a carrier or a `Headers` slot), not only "the body is a carrier". The capability is asymmetric in spelling: a `Headers` parameter on `get`, `input.headers` in a carrier on `post`. Both are the same `Headers` type with the same transport, rebuild and 500, and the asymmetry follows from `post`'s last position being its body. Typed function values spell `var Headers`. A `Headers` route copies each field twice per request.

**Revisit when:**
- `POST` without a body is decided: revisit whether a bodyless `post` takes a `Headers` slot as `get` does;
- the R1 transport changes (for example `_Call` carrying the `Request`): revisit whether the slot lends the request's `Headers` instead of rebuilding it (M3-012's condition, now for both header surfaces);
- `get_headers_fail/typed_borrowed_headers_value.mojo` compiles: typed borrowed values convert, and the `var Headers` spelling rule can go (with `registration_fail/typed_value_to_owned_slot.mojo`);
- applications repeat the same field parsing with a 400, or ask for typed header values: revisit `FromHeaders` (candidate 3), with `Headers` conforming to it so the shapes chosen here stay; compile-time names stay under M3-012's `struct_level_assert.mojo` condition;
- several route values, or a second request part beside the fields (cookies, the method), is proposed: revisit whether `Headers` stays one slot, last, and count it in the slot arity (`def(Int, Int, Headers)` is slot arity 3, eight overloads per method);
- applications need the fields before a route value: revisit position independence with its index and flag rules (1′).

**Next production slice (M3-017): typed `get` header access through a `Headers` slot.**
- `src/muntin/app.mojo`, exactly the spike's edits applied to production:
  - `_kind`: `A == Headers` returns a new kind `_HEADERS`, after `Request`;
  - `_get_rule`: the spike's `Headers` branch, after the no-kind check and before the two-`Int` check; `_post_rule`: `k1 == _OTHER or k1 == _HEADERS` where it reports a first slot of no kind; nothing else in either;
  - `_check`: a new code and assert `a get handler takes one Headers, as its last parameter`, and the no-kind `get` message reworded to `a get handler's parameter is an Int route value, the request Headers or, for a raw handler, the Request`; every other message unchanged;
  - `_slot`: an `A == Headers` branch that rebuilds the fields from the name and value pairs from `args[at]` on through `Headers.add` (a new private helper; a failure is `_Reject(500)`) and returns them through `_as[Headers, A]`;
  - `_route`: `headers=` is also true when the last slot is `Headers`; `_Route.headers`'s docstring says it marks a route whose handler receives the fields (a carrier or a `Headers` slot);
  - the module comment and docstrings that the slice makes false (on `get`'s shapes, typed routes receiving no header strings, the arity-2 `get` overloads accepting no handler), found by grep for `No such handler is`, `header strings only` and `receives no headers unless` (the `post` docstrings that `No such handler is` also finds stay true and unchanged).
- Unchanged: every overload signature and count and the `where` clause; `App.handle`'s code; `_Route`'s fields; `_carrier_fields`, `_raw_request`, `_json_status`; `_Erased`, `_Call`, `_Bound`, `_handler_storage.mojo`, `check_unsafe.sh` and the one `rebind_var`; `Headers`, `Request`, `Response`, `WithHeaders`, `State`; `testing.mojo`; `adapters/flare/muntin_flare.mojo`. No `post` behavior or message.
- Tests: `tests/test_get_headers.mojo`, the spike tests ported to production `app.get` (every case listed under Evidence), plus DX's new `get` example. Loopback in `adapters/flare/test_localhost_roundtrip.mojo`: a stateful `GET /signed/{id}` route (`State`, `Int`, `Headers`) over HTTP/1.1: repeated `X-A`/`x-a` fields in order with their casing (200), the handler's 401 without the credential and a route-value 400, each status and body equal to `App.handle`.
- Fixtures: `tests/get_headers_api_fail` on production, registered in `check.sh`: `headers_before_route_value`, `two_headers`, `headers_then_body`, `typed_borrowed_headers_value` (ported from the spike's) and the five `post_*` fixtures (moved). For each, the PR says whether it fails for its documented reason only with the slice (built against M3-016's `src`: the first two fail with the old no-kind message; the others fail with the same text). `storage_fail/string_param_handler` and `registration_api_fail/slot_misuse_names_the_rule` are re-pinned to the reworded message.
- Diagnostics: every fixture of the slice's base built against the base's and the slice's `src` and compared in full, normalized as above; expected: identical except the two re-pins.
- Mutations (scratch copies of `src/muntin`), each red: the kind removed; the flag not set; the rebuild answered 400; `def(Headers, Int)` accepted; two `Headers` accepted; the rebuild read from index 0; a condition on the guard only; the `Headers` branch first in `_get_rule`; the `_post_rule` edit removed (`post_headers_before_body_with_route_value` compiles); `Headers` accepted as a `post` body.
- Spike retirement: `tests/get_headers_spike.mojo`, `tests/test_spike_get_headers.mojo` and `tests/get_headers_fail` are deleted (their production successors above), with `check.sh`'s fixture list and the `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` revisit index updated. This record keeps what the spike measured.
- Docs: `docs/DX.md` (the `get` shapes and rule messages, a typed `get` header example, and every statement that typed `get` handlers read no fields, found by grep for `` typed `get` ``, `read no fields` and the old message text); `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Current architecture" (registration surface, request handling and transport through the box, Headers, other limits); `docs/SPEC.md` and `README.md` (the capability moves to shipped); `AGENT_PROGRESS.md`. No new record unless the slice cannot be implemented as written.
- Done: the above; `check.sh` (its unsafe confinement step runs `check_unsafe.sh`), `test.sh`, `check_flare.sh` and `git diff --check` exit 0, and production's only `rebind_var` is still the one in `_as`; CI `verify` and `flare` pass; a fresh-context review finds no unresolved material issue.
- Not in the slice: a `Headers` slot on `post` or a bodyless `POST`, `Headers` in another position or more than once, a wrapper type, `FromHeaders` or any Muntin status for header values, compile-time header names, cookies, authentication, middleware, request-scoped injection, and any change to raw handlers, `WithHeaders`, `Headers` or `TestClient`.

### Production verification policy decision

Status: **decision** (PR #48; `src/muntin`, `adapters/`, `tests/`, `scripts/` and CI are unchanged in it, and it is settled from existing evidence, with no spike). It decides which recurring verification a production item needs. The case is M3-017 (PR #47): a small change whose verification ran the three suites locally, compared every fixture's compiler output and the new fixtures against the base, ran the slice's mutation list, and took two review rounds.

**Question.** Which of these mechanisms protect against something no other required check covers, and which repeat one? Settled by, for each mechanism: the failure class it targets, whether it caught anything in M3-017 (PR #47's Verification section) or in a contrasting item, and which other required check covers the same class.

**Where the cost came from.** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` required none of the heavy steps. Decision records prescribed them in their "Next production slice" (M3-016's Diagnostics, Mutations and Done lines, the pattern of M3-014's slice), and most production items since M2-006 added their own route to `adapters/flare/test_localhost_roundtrip.mojo`.

**Evidence, per mechanism:**

- `check.sh`. It holds protection no other check supplies: unsafe confinement and the architecture boundary are the only checks of their invariants, and the expected texts are the only pins of user-facing diagnostics. This decision keeps the script whole rather than weighing each of its steps. In M3-017 the expected texts carried the two re-pins and turned the acceptance and rule-order mutations red. CI runs it on both systems in a few minutes.
- `test.sh`. In M3-017 the tests turned the flag, rebuild and guard mutations red; no fixture covers runtime behavior. CI runs it.
- `check_flare.sh`. A `src` change can break the adapter's build, which only this script sees, and CI runs it on every code pull request. M3-017's new route (`GET /signed/{id}`) proved nothing beyond `App.handle`: the adapter converts every field of every request (`to_muntin_headers`) and does not route, `App.handle` decides per route whether the fields reach the handler, and M3-005's route already proves on the wire that repeated fields keep their order and casing.
- Base-wide diagnostic comparison. In M3-017 it found nothing: every fixture but the two re-pins was byte-identical, and those two differed only in the message line the re-pins check. What it can see that the expected texts cannot is the rest of the output: call-chain frames and overload notes. A rule's branch or message is evaluated in `_check`, after overload resolution (the `where` clauses constrain only the result type), so it changes only the message line. Where overloads or the call chain changed, the comparison found what the pins could not: in M3-015, 77 fixtures gained `_check` frames and 14 changed their candidate notes (that record, "Diagnostics"); in M3-003, 8 failing `get` calls gained the stateful candidates' notes.
- New fixtures against the base. It separated M3-017's fixtures that pin the change (four fail on the base with the old message) from regression pins (the rest fail with the same text). One build per new fixture.
- Mutations. The slice's ten were red, as M3-016 had measured them on its spike, which modeled production's registration, and on a scratch copy of production; production repeated those measurements on the code the slice described. The one that found a gap came from the review asking which DX statement had no pin: with both `Headers` placeholder checks returning `_OK`, every test and fixture still passed, though DX said the fixtures prove those rules.
- Fresh-context review. It found that gap (material), two stale comments and the README point; the review scoped to the fixes found nothing. M3-017 changed the public API, so section 5's trigger already covered it.

**Candidates:**

| Candidate | Result |
|---|---|
| 1. keep: slices go on prescribing local suites, a base-wide comparison, a mutation list and a loopback route | rejected: in M3-017 the comparison, the slice mutations and the route repeated measured results and found nothing; the gap was found by a question none of them asks |
| 2. keep what is unique everywhere; make the rest conditional on a stated trigger | **chosen** |
| 3. drop the base-wide comparison and mutations | rejected: M3-015 shows what the comparison alone sees when overloads or the call chain change, and M3-017 shows a targeted mutation finding an unpinned claim |

**Selected** (written into `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` sections 2, 3 and 5):

- Universal for a pull request that changes code, unchanged: CI's `verify` and `flare` on both systems (all of `check.sh`, `test.sh` and `check_flare.sh`) and `git diff --check`, as the merge gate; tests and fixtures for the new behavior; for each behavior, diagnostic or invariant the item newly documents as guaranteed, executable evidence that fails if the claim stops being true (rationale, costs and implementation descriptions are not such claims); each new must-not-build fixture that pins the change built against the base's `src`.
- Conditional, each with its trigger:
  - base-wide comparison: a change that can alter compiler output outside the checked expected texts (overload resolution or the candidate set, the signatures candidate notes print, instantiation or call-chain frames, the compiler itself). Examples: a `get` or `post` overload added or removed, an overload's signature or `where` clause, `_check`'s parameters or the call chain to it, the Mojo version. It fires for M3-003 and M3-015, not for M3-017;
  - a local `check_flare.sh` and loopback coverage of the changed wire behavior, preferably by extending an existing case: a change that can reach the wire (`adapters/`, `compat/`, the `Request`, `Response` or `Headers` types or their conversion, how `App.handle` reads the request or builds the response, a backend-dependent limit or status). A new route is a means, not a requirement;
  - a mutation: a documented guarantee that no test or fixture plainly targets. No count;
  - fresh-context review: section 5's trigger, unchanged. The reviewer also checks newly documented guarantees against executable evidence; if the full round produces fixes, one review scoped to them follows.
- A slice may require more only for a concrete risk the list does not cover, and names that risk. The check belongs to that item; later items inherit it only if section 3 is changed to say so. Old one-off checks are not carried forward by default.
- A decision item is settled with evidence suited to its question: existing records and pull requests where they suffice, spikes and fixtures where compiler or runtime behavior must be measured. This decision is the first case of the former.
- No longer required: local runs of the three suites as a completion criterion (CI on the final HEAD is the evidence), a slice's mutation list repeating its decision's measurements, and a loopback route for each handler shape.

**Invariants not weakened.** Unsafe confinement (one `rebind_var[` in `src/muntin`), the architecture boundary, every expected diagnostic text, the known-gap and toolchain-gap fixtures, the State guarantee's pins, and `ci-ok` requiring every suite on both systems. No test, fixture, script or CI job changes.

**Cost and risk.** A change outside the comparison trigger that alters frames or notes reaches `main` unnoticed; the rule message, which DX documents, is still checked. The trigger is judged by the author and the reviewer, so a missed trigger is the main risk. An adapter defect that depends on the route would need loopback coverage for that route; the adapter does not depend on the route today. Without required local runs, a failure can show up in CI after the push instead of before it.

**Revisit when:** a defect reaches `main` that a skipped base-wide comparison, loopback case or slice mutation would have caught; the adapter starts depending on the route (for example, converting header fields only for routes that read them); or a production item outside the trigger still needs the base-wide comparison to settle a question.

**Next action: deferral.** There is no production slice: this record's pull request writes the policy into `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, and the next production item follows it. Revisit under the conditions above.

### Route-value decoding and String route values decision (M3-018)

Status: **decision** (M3-018, PR #49; `src/muntin`, `adapters/` and `tests/` are unchanged in it). It decides that percent-decoding belongs to the capture of a route value rather than to its type's conversion, reopens M2 so that `Int` values follow that rule, and decides what a `String` route value then receives, so that `/users/{name}` is routable. M2-016 named non-`Int` route values the most visible gap; M3-014 decided that raw `String` is a route-value type and never a body, and that adding it is one slot kind and converter, no overload.

**Why this candidate.** Of SPEC's remaining candidates:
- Route-value decoding and `String` route values: every route that names something other than a number (`/users/{name}`, `/tags/{slug}`, `/search?{q}`) is unreachable by a typed handler today, and raw literals declare no placeholder, so it is not reachable by a raw one either. Whether a route value is raw or decoded text is a semantic default that cannot change later without silently changing what an unchanged handler receives, and later route-value items (several values, path and query together, optional values, other value types) build on it.
- Middleware: more leverage overall, but `App.handle` is already the one seam and no handler shape depends on what wraps it, so postponing it builds nothing on a wrong seam; its design is also the largest and least settled on Mojo 1.1.0 (no captures, no existentials).
- `put`, `patch`, `delete`: M3-014 settled the structure (six arity overloads each over the shared rules); there is little design uncertainty left, so it is production work, not a decision.
- `POST` without a body and `FromHeaders`: both are M3-016 revisit conditions whose triggers have not fired.
- `app.run()`/lifecycle: serving belongs to the backend (SPEC, M2-016 item 1) and depends on Flare's server API more than on Muntin's.
- Several route values: a rule change that is cheaper after the value types are settled; `(Int, Int, B)` on `post` is slot arity 3, eight overloads.

**Question.** When Muntin captures a typed route value from a `{name}` path segment or a `{key}` query item, is percent-decoding part of the capture or of the destination type's conversion? If the capture owns it, should the existing `Int` semantics reopen accordingly? And what exact values, failures, shapes and diagnostics does adding `String` then produce?

**What settles it:**
- where decoding belongs: whether the alternatives make a handler's type decide how its URL is read, what each costs existing `Int` routes (which inputs change status, which proven DX statements and tests change), and what later value types would parse;
- the value's content: what clients can send (over Flare a request target carries only `!`..`~`, so any other byte reaches Muntin percent-encoded), the encodings in RFC 3986 section 2.1 and the `+` rule of the WHATWG URL Standard's `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` parser, and Mojo 1.1.0's behavior when a `String` is built from decoded bytes that are not UTF-8 (measured);
- shapes, statuses and messages: scratch copies of production with a `String` kind and each location of the decoding, built and run with `check.sh` (which expected texts change), `test.sh` (which existing tests change) and probes of each status, and a loopback probe through Flare for decoding exactly once;
- what the M2 contract says about each changed behavior, and whether a change follows M2-016's reopen conditions.

**Boundaries:**
- `Int` route values (conversion, the set of inputs answered 400, nothing decoded) change only through an explicit M2 reopen recorded here; query keys compared byte for byte; `Request.path` and `Request.query` raw; matching on the raw path; raw handlers;
- M3-014: `String` is a route value and never a body;
- the registration structure: six overloads per method, the `where` clause, the rule guard, one `rebind_var`, the storage module and unsafe confinement;
- every existing diagnostic, unless this record names the change and why;
- the M2 contract's 400/404/500 boundary (SPEC, "M2 completion contract"); a change to it reopens M2.

**Candidates to measure:** where decoding belongs (every route value at capture, reopening M2 for `Int`; `String` only; capture with `Int` kept raw; no decoding), and, under the chosen one, the empty value, the decoding rules (strict or WHATWG's, `+`, keys), control characters, where the decoder runs, a Muntin text type and a conversion trait for application route-value types, and deferral.

**Selected: a route value is percent-decoded once, when it is captured, and then converted; `String` becomes a route-value kind beside `Int`.** Decoding belongs to the capture, not to a type: the `Int` and `String` handlers on one route receive the same decoded text, and a later value type parses decoded text too. Because `Int` values were undecoded, this reopens M2 for one statement (below). It adds no overload, no public type and no injected kind.

```mojo
def profile(name: String) -> String:
    return "profile " + name

def search(q: String) -> String:
    return "results for " + q

app.get["/users/{name}"](profile)
# GET /users/alice        -> 200 "profile alice"
# GET /users/J%C3%B6rg    -> 200 "profile Jörg"
# GET /users/a+b          -> 200 "profile a+b"     (`+` is literal in a path)
# GET /users/%zz, /users/%FF -> 400 "Bad Request"  (bad escape; not UTF-8)
app.get["/search?{q}"](search)
# GET /search?q=mojo+lang -> 200 "results for mojo lang"
# GET /search?q=a%2Bb     -> 200 "results for a+b"
# GET /search, /search?q=, /search?q=1&q=2 -> 400 "Bad Request"
# and, for the existing `app.get["/items?{limit}"](list_items)`:
# GET /items?limit=%31%30 -> 200 "items 10"        (400 before this decision)
```

- **Shapes.** A `String` parameter goes wherever an `Int` route value goes, once: `get` `def(String)` and `def(String, Headers)`; `post` `def(String, B)` (`B` a `FromBody`, a `Json[T]` or a `WithHeaders[B2]`); each also after a leading `State[S]`. The route literal declares exactly one `{name}` segment or `{key}` item for it, as for `Int`; binding is positional. Results and errors are unchanged.
- **The kind** is decided by exact type equality, `A == String`, beside `Int`, `Request` and `Headers` (`String` is origin-free, so `_as[String, A]`, which `_respond` already uses for results, is exact). `StaticString`, `StringSlice` and application types are not route values. M3-014's rule stands: `String` is never a body, and `def(String)` on `post` keeps today's message.
- **A placeholder binds one non-empty value, whatever its type.** A path segment is never empty (an empty segment does not match, 404), and an empty query value (`?q=` or `?q`) is 400 for every route value, `String` included. Telling an empty value from an absent one belongs to the optional and default values item. 400 keeps today's M2 boundary; a later optional or default values decision can deliberately broaden the accepted set without invalidating any value accepted before, while a value now and 400 later would reject requests that work today.
- **Path capture:** the matched segment, percent-decoded (RFC 3986 section 2.1): each `%` followed by two hex digits (either case) becomes that byte, every other byte is kept, and `+` is literal. Matching runs first on the raw path, so `%2F` decodes to `/` inside one value and never splits a segment. Nothing is normalized (`..` and `%2E` are values like any other).
- **Query capture:** the value `App.handle` already finds by key, then `+` becomes a space, then the same percent-decoding. Keys are matched as before: undecoded, byte for byte, missing or duplicated 400.
- **Muntin's decoding, not a form parser.** Only the `+` rule comes from the `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` format, which is how `URLSearchParams` and HTML forms send a space. The WHATWG URL Standard's parser for that format decodes differently in three ways, each deliberate here (its binding also differs: `URLSearchParams` gives `""` for `?q=` and keeps duplicates, where Muntin's rules above answer 400): it keeps a `%` not followed by two hex digits (`?q=%zz` is `%zz` there, 400 here), it replaces bytes that are not UTF-8 with U+FFFD (`?q=%FF` is `"�"` there, 400 here, because a lossy value would give `%FE` and `%FF` the same name), and it decodes names too (`?%71=x` has the name `q` there; here it does not match `{q}`).
- **Invalid values, a closed set, each 400 before the handler:** an empty value, a `%` not followed by two hex digits, and decoded bytes that are not UTF-8 (Mojo 1.1.0's `String(from_utf8=)` raises on them, measured). Anything else that decodes to valid UTF-8 is a value, NUL and other control characters included (`%00`, `%0A`, `%09`): which text an application accepts is the application's to decide (RFC 3986 section 7.3), as a missing header field is. Decoding validates the encoding, not the application's safety: a handler that passes a route value to a filesystem, a C API, a database or another interpreter validates the characters that destination requires.
- **Conversion after decoding:** a `String` receives the decoded text; an `Int` parses it with M2's unchanged rule (optional `-`, ASCII digits, `Int` range), so `%34%32` is 42 and `%2D7` is -7, while `+1`, `%2B1`, `%20`, `%zz` and `%2534` are still 400.
- **Where it runs:** in `App.handle`, where the values are captured: after `_match` has filled the raw arguments with the path captures, each is replaced by its decoded text, and the `_query_value` result is decoded before it is appended; one `try` around both answers 400. `App.handle` knows whether a value came from the path or the query, so no flag reaches the adapters, and the adapters, the overloads' signatures and `_Erased` are unchanged. `_slot`'s `String` branch copies the decoded text into the handler's value; its `Int` branch parses as before.
- **Order:** 404 → query value missing or duplicated, then any value empty, a bad escape or not UTF-8, 400 (`App.handle`) → `Int` conversion 400 (adapter) → body steps (415, 413, field rebuild 500, body 400) or the `Headers` rebuild → handler → result. Each status and its position relative to the body steps are as for `Int` today.
- **Ownership:** a fresh `String` per request, moved into the handler, which declares `name: String` or `var name: String`; `mut name: String` selects no overload, as for `Int`. A typed function value spells it `var String` (`def(String) thin raises Never -> String` is the toolchain's `TODO: function type conversions between closures not supported yet`, as for `Int` and `Headers`), and a generic helper forwards a `String` handler through M3-015's edge 4 (measured).
- **Raw handlers and `Request`:** unchanged and undecoded. Raw literals declare no placeholder, so a raw route captures nothing; `req.path` and `req.query` stay the text received, and an application that must see the encoded form (`%2F` versus `/`) uses a raw handler.

**M2 reopen.** M2 says nothing is percent-decoded: `docs/DX.md` ("Proven vs. target status") states it for path values ("Percent-encoding is not decoded") and for query values ("`limit=%31%30` is not `10`", proven by `tests/test_app.mojo`, where `/items?limit=%31%30` is 400). Decoding `Int` values changes that M2 semantics rather than adding to it, which [M2 closure (M2-016)](#m2-closure-m2-016) makes a reopen needing its own explicit decision; this record is that decision. M2-016's other condition, a proven DX statement that stops passing its test, does not arise as a defect: the slice changes both DX statements and the test together, to the decoded behavior. What changes for `Int`: a value that contains at least one valid escape and whose decoded text satisfies M2's `Int` rule (`%34%32`, `%2D7`, `-%37`, `?limit=%31%30`) is no longer 400; it gets what the same value written in plain digits gets (the body steps, then the handler's result or error, so a raising handler's 500 or a `Json[T]` body's 415 where those apply). Escaped text that decodes to something the `Int` rule rejects (`%2D%2D7`, `%2B1`, `%20`, a number out of range) is still 400. Measured: `/items?limit=%31%30` is the one existing test whose outcome changes; no other existing test showed a semantic change. No value that was accepted changes meaning (an accepted `Int` value has no `%` and no `+`), and nothing that was answered without a 400 now gets one. What stays: every invalid route value is still 400 before the handler, the "missing, duplicated, empty or invalid query value" rule, binding, results, the raw escape hatch, the backend seam and every M2 signature. The slice amends the M2 contract for this, as M3-015 did, and updates both DX statements in the same pull request.

**Rules and diagnostics** (measured):
- `_kind`: `A == String` is a new kind, after `Headers`. `_get_rule` and `_post_rule` treat it as a route value; a `String` as a body falls through to the existing `FromBody` messages.
- New messages, only for shapes with a `String`, where the `Int` text would be false: `handler takes one String parameter; route must declare exactly one path or query parameter`, `handler takes one String parameter and the request body; route must declare exactly one path or query parameter`, and `a get handler takes at most one route value` (two route values with a `String` among them; `def(Int, Int)` keeps `a get handler takes at most one Int route value`).
- Two messages list what a parameter may be and would become false, so they are reworded, as M3-016 reworded the first: `a get handler's parameter is an Int or String route value, the request Headers or, for a raw handler, the Request` and `a post handler's parameter before the body is an Int or String route value`. They re-pin `registration_api_fail/slot_misuse_names_the_rule` and `get_headers_api_fail/post_headers_before_body{,_with_route_value}`. `storage_fail/string_param_handler` (a `String` parameter on `/greet/{name}`) compiles and is retired; its comment already said "`String` is no slot kind yet".
- Every other expected text is unchanged.

Probed shapes, built against `main`'s `src` and the scratch copy (`/x` has no placeholder). On `main` each shape in the first seven rows fails with `get`'s no-kind message or `post`'s before-the-body message, as a `String` is no kind there; the last two rows are unchanged:

| Shape | Selected |
|---|---|
| `get` `def(String)` on `/x/{a}`; `def(String, Headers)` on `/x?{a}`; `post` `def(String, B)` and `def(State[P], String, B)` on `/x/{a}` | registers |
| `get` `def(String)` on `/x`, `/x/{a}/{b}`, `/x/{a}?{b}`; `def(String, Headers)` and `def(State[P], String)` on `/x` | `handler takes one String parameter; ...` |
| `get` `def(String, String)`, `def(Int, String)`, `def(String, Int)`, `def(State[P], String, String)`; `def(String, Int)` on `/x` | `a get handler takes at most one route value` |
| `get` `def(Headers, String)` on `/x/{s}` | `a get handler takes one Headers, as its last parameter` |
| `get` `def(StaticString)`; `post` `def(Headers, B)`, `def(StaticString, B)` | the two reworded messages |
| `post` `def(String, B)` on `/x` and `/x/{a}?{b}`, `def(String, Headers)` on `/x`, `def(State[P], String, B)` on `/x` | `handler takes one String parameter and the request body; ...` |
| `post` `def(String, String)` on `/x/{a}`; `def(String, Int)`; `def(String, Request) -> Response` | `the handler's last parameter is the request body; ...`; `Int is a route-value type, ...`; `Request is the whole request, not a body; ...` (the `Int` shapes' messages) |
| unchanged: `get` `def(Int, Int)`, `def(String, Note)`, `def(String, Request) -> Response`, stateful handler without its state; `post` `def(String)` on `/x` and on `/x/{a}`, `def(Int, String)`, `def(Note, String)`, `def(State[P], String)` | the message each had |
| unchanged: `get` `def(mut String)`, `def(String, String, Headers)`, `def(String) -> Int`; typed `def(String) thin raises Never -> String` value | `no matching method in call to 'get'`; the `TODO` conversion error |

**Candidates.** The first four answer where decoding belongs and are compared head on; the rest answer the sub-questions.

| Candidate | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| A. decode every route value at capture, then convert; reopen M2 for `Int` | capture decoding changes the existing `/items?limit=%31%30` case from 400 to the behavior of plain `10`, and no other existing test changes; targeted probes confirmed the `String` and `Int` behavior stated above | **chosen** |
| B. `String` decodes, `Int` stays raw (decoding as the `String` conversion) | expressible (decoding in the `String` slot; the same diagnostics as A) | rejected: the handler's type would decide whether the URL is decoded (`%34%32` is `"42"` for `String`, 400 for `Int`), and every later value type would reopen the question of raw or decoded input |
| C. capture is decoded text, with `Int` kept raw as a recorded M2 exception | not built (B's behavior with A's principle) | rejected: it states A's rule and keeps B's inconsistency, for a compatibility gain of 400s on escaped digits only |
| D. raw text for every route value | needs no measurement: B's kinds and rules without the decoder. Over Flare a target carries only `!`..`~`, so every space or non-ASCII character in a name arrives as `%XX` and the handler would receive `J%C3%B6rg`; decoding later would change the bytes an unchanged `def(String)` receives, with no compile error | rejected |
| empty query value as `String("")` | not built | rejected: a placeholder binds a non-empty value (above), and a later decision can broaden 400 to a value without invalidating values accepted before |
| WHATWG's parser semantics (`%zz` kept, U+FFFD, names decoded) | not built | rejected: lossy values collide, and decoding names would change which requests match `Int` query routes; only its `+` rule is taken |
| `+` literal in query values too (RFC 3986 only) | not built | rejected: `URLSearchParams` and forms encode a space as `+`, so `?q=a+b` would reach the handler as `a+b` |
| rejecting NUL or other control characters | not built | rejected: Muntin cannot know which text an application accepts; the invalid set stays closed |
| decoding query keys | not built | deferred: it would change which requests match existing `Int` routes and makes a literal `{a+b}` unmatchable; revisit condition below |
| decoding in `_slot`, with a compile-time `query` flag on the arity adapters | expressible: the adapters gain a parameter only to learn where a value came from, which `App.handle` already knows | rejected |
| a Muntin text type as the slot (`Segment`, with raw and decoded access) | not built: M3-016's wrapper candidate measured the same pattern for `Headers` (no material gain); it adds a public type and a hop, and M3-014 and DX section 3 already spell the value `String` | rejected |
| a conversion trait for application route-value types (`UserId` from a segment) | not built: M2-005 names it as the case that breaks disjointness with `FromBody` (a type conforming to both is classified by `_kind`'s order), and M3-016's trait candidate measured the same precedence problem. `String` cannot conform to a Muntin trait, so it is a kind by equality under any later trait; such a type would parse decoded text | rejected now; additive later, beside the `String` kind |
| defer | `/users/{name}` stays unreachable by a typed or raw handler | rejected |

**Measured premise: Flare passes the encoded target through.** Decoding in Muntin is correct only if the backend does not decode first, and no loopback case sends `%XX`. A raw-TCP probe through Flare v0.11.0 confirmed that the backend keeps the target encoded: `/users/%2541` reaches Muntin encoded and the one decoding in Muntin gives `%41`, and `/i/%2534` is 400 for an `Int`.

**Invariants:**
- A route value is decoded exactly once, in `App.handle`, from the raw capture, before any conversion; `+` is a space only in a query value. Query keys, `Request.path`, `Request.query` and raw handlers are never decoded.
- A placeholder binds one non-empty value. The invalid values are exactly: empty, a bad escape, not UTF-8; each is 400 before the handler. No new status.
- `String` is a request slot kind by exact type equality; a route value is an `Int` or a `String`, at most one per handler; `String` is never a body.
- No overload, adapter signature, `_Route` field, storage or unsafe change; one `rebind_var`.

**Cost.** M2 reopens: an `Int` value written with escapes is accepted (the M2 reopen paragraph has the exact scope). Query keys are matched undecoded while values are decoded. The decoding is Muntin's own and is not what `URLSearchParams` parses (three documented decoding differences). Three route-value messages exist in an `Int` and a `String` form so that every `Int` message stays byte-identical; two existing messages are reworded and one fixture is retired. Each route value costs one decoded copy per request, `Int` included, and a `String` value a second copy at the slot.

**Revisit when:**
- an application must tell `%2F` from `/` or see the encoded text in a typed handler (a raw handler can today): revisit a raw-text form;
- applications need lossy decoding, a `+` kept in a query value, or control characters rejected: revisit the closed invalid set and the `+` rule;
- a route literal needs a query key that clients encode (`{tags[]}` arrives from `URLSearchParams` as `tags%5B%5D`): revisit decoding keys;
- an application-defined route-value type is proposed: revisit the conversion trait with M2-005's disjointness condition; it parses decoded text;
- a backend delivers a decoded target: the seam must carry the raw target, or decoding moves;
- optional or default query values are decided: revisit whether an empty value stays 400;
- several route values are decided: the `at most one route value` messages and the `Int`/`String` message pairs are replaced by that item's rules.

**Next production slice (M3-019): route-value decoding and `String` route values.**
- `src/muntin/app.mojo` only:
  - `_kind`: `A == String` returns a new kind `_TEXT`, after `Headers`;
  - `_get_rule` and `_post_rule`: `_TEXT` as a route value beside `_INT`, with the three new rule codes. On `get`, after the existing `State`, `Request`, body, no-kind and `Headers`-first checks: two `Int`s keep `_GET_TWO_VALUES`; any other two route values are `_GET_ONE_VALUE`, before the placeholder count; then one route value (alone or before `Headers`) needs exactly one placeholder, with the `Int` or the `String` placeholder message. On `post`, a first-slot `String` before the body takes the `Int` path, with the `String` placeholder message; a `String` in the body position reaches the existing `FromBody` codes. The oracle is the probe table above: each probed shape gets the listed result;
  - `_check`: the three new messages and the two rewordings above; every other message unchanged;
  - a private decoder (raw text and whether it is a query value): empty, a bad escape or non-UTF-8 raise; `+` a space only for a query value; `String(from_utf8=)` builds the result;
  - `App.handle`, typed routes only: after `_match` (itself unchanged) fills the raw arguments, each path capture is replaced in place by its decoded text, and the `_query_value` result is decoded before it is appended; the existing `try` around the query lookup is widened to cover both, still answering 400;
  - `_slot`: an `A == String` branch that returns a copy of `args[at]` through `_as[String, A]`; the `Int` branch unchanged;
  - the docstrings and comments the slice makes false, found by grep for `route value`, `Int`, `def(Int`, `decod`, `+`, `400` and `query value` in `app.mojo` (the overloads' and `_slot`'s descriptions of a route value, `_get_rule`'s and `_post_rule`'s lists of accepted shapes and their rule-order notes, `App.handle`'s docstring on what it passes and what it answers 400; `_query_value`'s "Nothing is percent-decoded" stays true of that function and is kept; `_as`'s list of origin-free types already names `String`).
- Unchanged: every overload's signature, their count and the `where` clause; the adapters (`_call_*`) and their parameters; `_match`, `_query_value` and `_parse_int` themselves; `_Route`; `_handler_storage.mojo`, the one `rebind_var`, `check_unsafe.sh`; `Request`, `Response`, `Headers`, `WithHeaders`, `State`, `Json`; `testing.mojo`; `adapters/flare/muntin_flare.mojo` (only its loopback test is extended, below). Every existing message other than the two rewordings; every `Int` behavior other than the reopen above.
- Tests: `tests/test_string_route_values.mojo` through `App.handle` and `TestClient`: path decoding (`%20`, UTF-8, `%2F` inside a value, `+` literal, `..`, `%00`), query decoding (`+`, `%2B`, `%26`), each 400 (bad escape, truncated escape, non-UTF-8, empty query value) and the 404 for an empty segment; `Int` decoding (`%34%32`, `%2D7`, `-%37`, a query value) and the `Int` inputs still 400 (`+1`, `%2B1`, `%20`, `%zz`, `%2D%2D7`); one decoding, not two, with expected values: `/users/%2541` reaches a `String` handler as `%41`, and `%2534` is 400 for an `Int`; raw handlers undecoded; `Headers`, `State`, a `post` body, `Json[T]` and `WithHeaders[B]` after a `String` with the route value's 400 first; `ToResponse`, `ToErrorResponse`; first registration wins; a typed `var String` value and generic forwarding; DX's new examples. In `tests/test_app.mojo`, `/items?limit=%31%30` moves from the 400 list to an accepted case (`items 10`), the reopen's executable evidence.
- Fixtures, in a new `tests/string_route_api_fail` registered in `check.sh`: the three new messages, each stateless and stateful (stateful: `def(State[P], String)` on `/x` and `def(State[P], String, String)` on `/x/{a}` on `get`, `def(State[P], String, B)` on `/x` on `post`); `def(String)` on `post` on a route with a placeholder (`handler takes only the request body; ...`, M3-014's "never a body" where a route value could bind); `def(StaticString)` on `/x/{a}` (the reworded no-kind message: not a route value); the typed borrowed `String` value (`TODO`). Each is built against `main`'s `src` and the pull request says whether it fails there with the same text. `compile_fail/post_string_body.mojo` already pins `def(String)` on `post` without a placeholder; its comment cites M3-014 instead of a future text body. The two rewordings re-pin their three fixtures; `storage_fail/string_param_handler.mojo` is deleted.
- Verification: the slice changes how `App.handle` reads the request, so `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` section 3's wire trigger applies: a local `check_flare.sh` before pushing, and loopback coverage, here for the one risk this item adds, a backend that decodes the target before Muntin (values decoded twice). In `adapters/flare/test_localhost_roundtrip.mojo`, extend `test_typed_route_over_localhost_matches_test_client` with a `String` path route and a `String` query route on paths no existing route shadows (`users_app()` registers `/users/{id}` first, so not `/users/{name}`), and targets that tell one decoding from two (`%2541`, `a%2Fb`, `a+b`, `?q=a+b%2B`) plus the existing `/users/{id}` route with `%34%32` and `%2534`, each with its expected status and body (`%41`; 400) and equal to `TestClient`, as the existing entries are. The base-wide diagnostic comparison is not required: no overload's signature, `where` clause, adapter or the call chain to `_check` changes.
- Docs: `docs/DX.md` (sections 2, 3 and 4: route values decoded at capture, the non-empty principle, the closed invalid set and that decoding validates the encoding, not the application's safety (the destination's characters are the handler's to check), the three decoding differences from WHATWG form parsing and its binding differences, `limit=%31%30` now `10` and the path statement that percent-encoding is not decoded; the registration rules; every quote of the two reworded messages, found by grep, section 9 included; the `String` example of a rejected parameter; every statement that route values are `Int` only or that nothing is decoded, scoped to keys and `Request`); `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Current architecture" ("When M2 reopens" stays true as written and needs no change; registration surface, the "`Headers` slot on `get`" bullet on `post` messages, request handling and its table, transport through the box, other limits) and a revisit index row for `tests/string_route_api_fail`; `docs/SPEC.md` (a paragraph after the M2 contract, as M3-015's: "M3-019 amended the contract (decided by M3-018)", stating that route values are decoded at capture and that escaped `Int` values are accepted; `String` route values go only in the M3 table, as shipped, and the "more route values" candidate drops its M3-018/M3-019 sentence); `AGENT_PROGRESS.md`.
- Not in the slice: several route values, path and query values together, optional or default values, other value types or a conversion trait, decoding query keys, a raw-text type, and any change to raw handlers.

### HTTP methods decision (M3-020)

Status: **decision** (M3-020, PR #51; `src/muntin`, `adapters/` and `tests/` are unchanged in it). It decides which request methods beyond `GET` and `POST` an application can register, under which spelling and with which handler shapes, so that a typed application can update and delete resources. Today every other method is 404 whatever the application registers, typed or raw.

**Why this candidate.** Of SPEC's remaining candidates:
- More HTTP methods (`put`, `patch`, `delete`): an application cannot answer `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` at all. No typed or raw handler can register for them, and the client chooses the method, so no route design works around it. A small typed framework whose API covers create and read but not update and delete is incomplete. M3-014 settled the structure (each method its own arity overloads over the shared rules, within a per-method note budget), and M3-018 called that "production work, not a decision". What it left open is public and hard to change once shipped: whether a `DELETE` handler may take a body, whether `PUT` and `PATCH` take exactly `post`'s shapes, the spelling, `TestClient`'s spelling for the new methods, and what a request gets when the path matches but the method does not. `DX.md` proves that `PUT /users` is 404 today. DEVELOPMENT.md section 2 needs a decision record to name a production slice.
- Several route values, path and query together: the closest competitor. Nested resources (`/users/{uid}/posts/{pid}`) and DX section 3's two-key search are unreachable today. But an application can choose its URLs (`/posts/{pid}`), and it cannot choose the method a client sends. Several values also carry a binding risk the methods do not: with no parameter-name reflection, two `Int` values bind by position only, and a swapped pair compiles. That needs its own decision, and it is not made easier by deciding it first. Deciding methods first does make several values dearer: slot arity 3 adds two overloads to every method name (M3-014), ten across five names instead of four across two. The cost is accepted because the overloads are mechanical copies under one rule set, while the missing methods block whole applications.
- Optional or default query values: useful on their own (`def list(limit: Int = 20)`), but what a default means for a registered handler is unresolved: a default cannot be expressed through a `def(var A)` slot, which has no default. Deciding them now gives less than restoring the missing methods.
- `POST` without a body, `FromHeaders`: M3-016 revisit conditions whose triggers have not fired.
- Middleware, lifecycle (`app.run()`), streaming: no handler shape depends on them, so postponing them builds nothing on a wrong seam, and each is larger and less settled on Mojo 1.1.0 (no captures, no existentials, serving owned by the backend).
- Schema/OpenAPI waits for a derived codec; parameter-name checking waits for function-parameter reflection.

**Question.** For which request methods beyond `GET` and `POST` does Muntin provide registration, under which public spelling, and with which handler shapes (route values, body, `Headers`, raw `Request`, `State`) and diagnostics for each? How does `TestClient` send them? And what does a request get when its path matches a route but its method matches none: 404 as today, or 405 with an `Allow` field?

**What settles it:**
- each method's semantics as HTTP defines them: RFC 9110 sections 9.3.4 (`PUT`), 9.3.5 (`DELETE`: content "has no generally defined semantics") and 15.5.6 (405 and its required `Allow` field), and RFC 5789 (`PATCH`). These decide which methods take a body, and whether one shape set can serve several methods;
- the spelling on Mojo 1.1.0: whether `put`, `patch` and `delete` compile as `App` method names and call as `app.delete[route](handler)`, and how a misuse's diagnostic names the method (measured with a compile probe);
- the backend premise: whether Flare v0.11.0 delivers `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` requests, with and without a body, to the handler with the method and body unchanged (measured with the smallest raw-TCP probe through Flare). The adapter copies the method verbatim, so no adapter change is expected; the probe settles whether Flare does too;
- the existing measurements: M3-014's C1 (a scratch `put` with its own overload set, `PUT /notes/7` answered through it, `POST` to it 404) and `tests/registration_fail/notes_are_per_method.mojo` (the note budget is per method name);
- for 405: what changes in `App.handle`'s scan and in the M2 contract ("No route match is 404"), and whether that follows M2-016's reopen conditions.

**Boundaries that do not change silently:**
- `get` and `post`: their six arity overloads each, their shapes, their rules and every existing diagnostic, unless this record names the change and why; the note budget per method name;
- the registration structure: generic-arity slots, the `where` clause on the result, the rule guard, one `rebind_var`, the storage module and unsafe confinement;
- `State` as the one registration-bound injected slot; the body and header rules (`FromBody`, `Json[T]`, `WithHeaders[B]`, the `Headers` slot on `get`);
- the raw `Request -> Response` escape hatch;
- route-value capture decoding (M3-018) and raw query keys;
- the backend seam at `App.handle`, with Flare out of the public API and of `src/muntin`;
- method matching byte for byte (`Request.method` as received), first registered matching route wins, a matched route never falls through;
- the M2 contract's 400/404/500 boundary (SPEC, "M2 completion contract"); a change to it, 405 included, reopens M2.

**Candidates to measure:**
- the method set: `put`, `patch` and `delete`; a subset first; or a generic `app.route[method, path]` entry for any method name, beside or instead of named methods;
- per method, the shapes: `post`'s (a required body last) or `get`'s (no body, a `Headers` slot last), with `DELETE` as the open case;
- `TestClient`: one method per new HTTP method, mirroring `get` and `post`; one generic `request(method, ...)`; or none (`app.handle(Request(...))`);
- a matched path with no matching method: 404 (unchanged) or 405 with `Allow` (M2 reopen);
- deferral.

**Selected: `put`, `patch` and `delete` as `App` methods, each with its own six arity overloads. `put` and `patch` take exactly `post`'s shapes, `delete` takes exactly `get`'s, and `TestClient` gains one method per new HTTP method.** A request whose path matches a route but whose method matches none stays 404. Nothing changes for `get`, `post`, `App.handle` or the adapters, so M2 does not reopen. No public type, slot kind or injected kind is added.

```mojo
def replace_user(id: Int, body: UserForm) -> String: ...              # `post`'s shapes
def rename_user(users: State[Users], id: Int, body: Json[Rename]) -> Json[User]: ...
def remove_user(id: Int, headers: Headers) raises Unauthorized -> String: ...  # `get`'s shapes

app.put["/users/{id}"](replace_user)
app.patch["/users/{id}"](rename_user, users)
app.delete["/users/{id}"](remove_user)
# PUT /users/7 "name=Ada"  -> the handler's answer (7 and the converted body)
# PUT /users/7 "Ada"       -> 400 "Bad Request" (from_body raised; handler not called)
# DELETE /users/abc        -> 400 "Bad Request" (before the handler)
# POST /users/7, HEAD /users/7, OPTIONS /users/7 -> 404 "Not Found" (no route of that method)

var client = TestClient(app)
client.put("/users/7", "name=Ada")
client.patch("/users/7", body, headers=h^)
client.delete("/users/7")
```

- **Shapes.** `put` and `patch` accept every shape `post` accepts and no other, with `post`'s rules: a required body last (a `FromBody`, a `Json[T]` or a `WithHeaders[B]`), at most one `Int` or `String` route value before it, the raw `def(Request) -> Response`, and each after a leading `State[S]` passed as the registration's second argument. `delete` accepts every shape `get` accepts and no other, with `get`'s rules: no body, at most one route value, a `Headers` slot last, the raw handler, each after a leading `State[S]`. Results, errors, route values (decoded at capture, M3-018), the `where` clause and binding are those of the method whose shapes it takes.
- **Why these shape sets.** `PUT` creates or replaces the target's state with the enclosed representation (RFC 9110 section 9.3.4) and `PATCH` applies the enclosed patch document (RFC 5789), so both need a body, as `post` does. Content received in a `DELETE` request "has no generally defined semantics" and "might lead some implementations to reject the request" (RFC 9110 section 9.3.5), word for word what section 9.3.1 says of `GET`, and `get` already takes no body. Admitting a body on `delete` later accepts more handlers and breaks none, while rejecting one later would break handlers that compile now. A handler that must read a `DELETE` body registers as a raw handler, which receives `req.body`.
- **Spelling.** `app.put[route](handler)`, `app.patch[route](handler)` and `app.delete[route](handler)`, with the state as the second argument, exactly as `get` and `post`. `put`, `patch` and `delete` compile as `App` method names and call with that syntax on Mojo 1.1.0 (measured). The route literal's grammar and checks are unchanged.
- **Matching.** A route of method `M` matches only a request whose `Request.method` is `M` byte for byte (`PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`), as today. Routes are scanned in registration order across all methods, and the first route whose method and path match handles the request and never falls through. A path that matches routes of other methods only is 404: Muntin's routes are independent `(method, path)` registrations, not resources that carry a set of allowed methods, and `App.handle` answers only whether a whole route matches. Its scan and the M2 contract's 404 (no route match) are unchanged.
- **Diagnostics.** Each Muntin message that names `get` or `post` names the method instead (`a delete handler takes no request body`, `a put handler takes the request body as its last parameter`, `a stateful raw patch handler takes State first, then only the Request, and returns Response`). Messages that name no method are shared unchanged (`handler takes one Int parameter; route must declare exactly one path or query parameter`). `get`'s and `post`'s messages stay byte-identical. A call that selects no overload is expected to be `no matching method in call to 'put'` (or `'patch'`, `'delete'`), as for `get` and `post`, with that method's six candidate notes, because the note budget is per method name (`tests/registration_fail/notes_are_per_method.mojo`). A `comptime assert` message may be a compile-time concatenation, and Mojo 1.1.0 prints it as the concatenated text (`constraint failed: a delete handler takes no request body`, measured), so one rule set with the method's name substituted serves every method of a shape family.
- **`TestClient`.** `put(target, body, *, headers=)`, `patch(target, body, *, headers=)` and `delete(target, *, headers=)`, with `post`'s and `get`'s parameters and M3-010's keyword-only, defaulted, moved-in `headers` (M3-010's revisit condition "`TestClient` gains another request method" is met by following that pattern). Each builds `Request("PUT", target, body, headers^)` (`"PATCH"`, or `"DELETE"` with an empty body) and calls `App.handle`, nothing else. A test that sends a `DELETE` body builds the `Request` and calls `App.handle`.
- **Raw handlers** register on each new method with the raw shape of its family and receive the whole `Request`, its `method` included.
- **Generic forwarding** reaches the new methods as it reaches `get` and `post`: the overloads have the same slots and the same `where` clause.

**Measured premise: Flare delivers the new methods.** A raw-TCP probe through Flare v0.11.0 sent `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` over HTTP/1.1, each with a 3-byte body and with none. Flare delivered each one to the handler, and the adapter's `to_muntin_request` gave a Muntin `Request` with the method, path and body unchanged, so no adapter change is needed. Flare answered `get`, `post`, `Get`, `put` and `delete` with 400 itself, before the handler, where `App.handle` would answer 404, and passed `FOO` and `OPTIONS` through; its HTTP/1.1 parser rejects any lowercase letter in a method unless `H1LeniencyConfig.allow_mixed_case_method` is set, which is off by default (`flare/http/_server/parse.mojo`). This is a current Flare limit for every method (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Other current limits and operational risks"): method handling differs between backends in ways route shapes do not, which is why the slice covers the new methods over loopback.

**Candidates.**

| Candidate | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A. `put`, `patch`, `delete`, each with six arity overloads; `put` and `patch` with `post`'s shapes, `delete` with `get`'s | **chosen** |
| B. a subset first (`delete` alone, or `put` and `patch`) | rejected: the three share every design choice above, and a subset leaves an application able to update but not delete, or the reverse. The slice is the same mechanical change per method |
| C. a generic `app.route[method, path](handler)` for any method, beside `get` and `post` or instead of new names | rejected: `PUT` would be registered differently from `POST` (`app.route["PUT", ...]` beside `app.post[...]`) while `get` and `post` keep their names, or, instead of named methods, every method including `GET` would need it, which changes M2's spelling. No current requirement needs a method outside the three (A7). (M3-014's C4b was the same entrypoint beside frozen `get` and `post`, rejected for giving one method two spellings; this case differs, and the reason here is consistency across methods.) Revisit condition below |
| D. `delete` with `post`'s shapes, or with an optional body | rejected: RFC 9110 gives `DELETE` content no semantics, `get` already rejects a body, and a raw `delete` handler reads one; admitting a body later is additive (above) |
| E. `TestClient`: one generic `request(method, target, body, headers=)`, or no new methods (`App.handle` only) | rejected: one method per HTTP method keeps a test's call reading like its registration (`client.delete` for `app.delete`); a generic client method is candidate C on the client side, and M3-010 kept a builder that opens arbitrary methods out of scope (its C2); `App.handle(Request(...))` already covers any other request |
| F. 405 with an `Allow` field when the path matches routes of other methods only | rejected now, because it needs a concept the router does not have. A 405 MUST carry `Allow`, "a list of the target resource's currently supported methods" (RFC 9110 section 15.5.6), so Muntin would have to treat the routes whose paths match as one resource and synthesize its method set: decide whether `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` are listed, how a raw route counts, and what a path that matches a typed route but would fail its route value answers. Those are the `HEAD`, `OPTIONS` and CORS questions, so 405 is decided with them or with another feature that needs a resource-level method set, not ahead of them. Until then 404 says what the router knows under its `(method, path)` model, that no route matched. RFC 9110 permits it here rather than requiring 405: 404 means the origin server "did not find a current representation for the target resource or is not willing to disclose that one exists" (section 15.5.5), and the resource exists for another method, so it is the second clause that covers this case. Its cost when it comes: it changes `App.handle`'s scan and the M2 contract's 404 for every method, `get` and `post` included (DX proves `PUT /users` is 404, and `tests/test_body.mojo` and `tests/test_int_body.mojo` assert 404 for `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE`), an M2 reopen under M2-016. The new methods do not raise that cost: method mismatch is already 404 for `get` and `post` routes. Revisit condition below |
| defer | rejected: no application can update or delete through Muntin, typed or raw |

**Not decided here:** `HEAD` and `OPTIONS`, whether answered automatically (a `HEAD` request through a `get` route, an `Allow` listing) or registered (RFC 9110 section 9.1: "All general-purpose servers MUST support the methods GET and HEAD"; today `HEAD` is 404); other methods (section 9.1 recommends 501 for a method the server does not implement; today they are 404). Each stays 404 until its own decision. Also not decided: bodyless typed `post`, `put` and `patch` handler shapes (`def()`, `def(V)`), which are compile errors (`def()` is `a put handler takes the request body as its last parameter`; `def(V)` gets the existing body messages) until M3-016's bodyless-`POST` condition is decided for all three. A request with an empty body is not that question: a typed body handler accepts it or answers it under the existing body rules (415 first for a `Json[T]` body without its `Content-Type`, then 400 if `B.from_body("")` raises), and a raw handler reads `req.body == ""`.

**Invariants:**
- One overload set per registration method name, six overloads each (slot arities 0 to 2, stateless and stateful), with the shared `where` clause; the note budget is per method name.
- `put` and `patch` accept exactly `post`'s shapes, `delete` exactly `get`'s, with the same rules in the same order; only the method's name in a message differs.
- Method matching is byte for byte, in registration order across methods; method mismatch is 404.
- `get`, `post`, `App.handle`, `_Route`, the adapters (`_call_*`), the storage module, the one `rebind_var` and the Flare adapter are unchanged.

**Cost.** Eighteen overloads, each a copy of its family's overload with the method name changed; a later slot arity 3 or 4 adds two overloads to every method name. A rule change to a shape family now applies to two or three methods. `DELETE` bodies reach only raw handlers. Method mismatch stays 404 without an `Allow` field.

**Revisit when:**
- an application needs a typed `DELETE` body: admit `post`'s shapes on `delete` (additive);
- an application needs a method outside `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`: revisit a generic entrypoint (candidate C) or another name;
- clients or a later feature (CORS preflight, middleware, `OPTIONS`) need 405 with `Allow` or another resource-level method set, or `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` are decided: revisit candidate F, introducing that view deliberately, with the M2 reopen;
- bodyless typed `post` handler shapes are decided: apply the decision to `put` and `patch`, or record why they differ;
- slot arity 3 or more is decided: every method name gains two overloads per arity (eight per name, forty across five names at slot arity 3), so re-evaluate copying an overload set per method against candidate C or generating the sets internally;
- a Flare upgrade, or another backend, changes how it delivers these methods or their bodies: re-run the measured premise.

**Next production slice (M3-021): `put`, `patch` and `delete`.**
- `src/muntin/app.mojo`:
  - `put` and `patch`: six overloads each, copies of `post`'s six with the method name (`"PUT"`, `"PATCH"`) in the `_check`, `_admits` and `_route` calls and in the docstrings; `delete`: six overloads, copies of `get`'s with `"DELETE"`. Each body has the same calls as its family's overload (`_check`, then `comptime if _admits` around the route append, `abort(_GUARD_DRIFT)` otherwise), so a rejected shape's diagnostic keeps the documented form: `function instantiation failed` at the enclosing function, with the registration call in the next note;
  - `_rule`: `"GET"` and `"DELETE"` use `_get_rule`, `"POST"`, `"PUT"` and `"PATCH"` use `_post_rule`; neither rule function changes;
  - `_check`: each message that names `get` (`_GET_STATE_ARGUMENT`, `_GET_RAW`, `_GET_STATE_RAW`, `_GET_BODY`, `_GET_SLOT_KIND`, `_GET_TWO_VALUES`, `_GET_HEADERS_LAST`, `_GET_ONE_VALUE`) or `post` (`_POST_NO_BODY`, `_POST_STATE_ARGUMENT`, `_POST_RAW`, `_POST_STATE_RAW`, `_POST_BODY_LAST`, `_POST_SLOT_KIND`) is built from the method's lowercase name, and is byte-identical for `GET` and `POST`; every other message unchanged. `_check`'s parameters and the call chain to it do not change;
  - the docstrings and comments the slice makes false, found by grep for `"GET"`, `"POST"`, `get`, `post` and `method` in `app.mojo` (`_rule`'s "`method` is `"GET"` or `"POST"`", the module comment's description of the registration surface).
- `src/muntin/testing.mojo`: `put`, `patch` and `delete` as above, beside `get` and `post`.
- Unchanged: `get` and `post` (signatures, count, bodies), `_get_rule`, `_post_rule`, `_kind`, `_slot`, the adapters, `_Route`, `App.handle`, `_handler_storage.mojo`, the one `rebind_var`, `check_unsafe.sh`; `Request`, `Response`, `Headers`, `WithHeaders`, `State`, `Json`; `adapters/flare/muntin_flare.mojo`. Every existing message and test outcome, the existing assertions that `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` are 404 on apps that register none included.
- Tests: `tests/test_methods.mojo` through `TestClient` and `App.handle`: on `put` and `patch`, `def(B)`, `def(V, B)` with `Int` and `String`, `Json[T]` (415, 413 and 400 before the handler), `WithHeaders[B]`, raw, and the stateful forms; on `delete`, `def()`, `def(V)`, `def(Headers)`, `def(V, Headers)`, raw and the stateful forms; route-value decoding and its 400 on a new method; `ToResponse` results, `raises` (500) and `ToErrorResponse`; one path with a route for each of the five methods, each request reaching its own handler; a method with no route on a matching path is 404, and a lowercase method is 404; first registration wins within a method; `TestClient`'s new methods equal `App.handle` for the same request, `headers=` included; a typed function value (`var` slots) and a generic helper forwarding to `put` and to `delete`; the slice's DX examples as written.
- Fixtures, in a new `tests/methods_api_fail` registered in `check.sh`: `def(B)` on `delete` (`a delete handler takes no request body`), `def()` on `put` (`a put handler takes the request body as its last parameter`), `def(B, B)` on `patch` (`a patch handler takes one request body, as its last parameter`), a stateful raw `delete` handler with an extra parameter (`a stateful raw delete handler takes State first, then only the Request, and returns Response`), `def(State[P], B)` on `put` without the state argument (`State is injected application state, not the request body; a stateful put handler takes State first and the body last, and the state is the registration's second argument`), and a handler with three request slots, no `State` and no state argument, on `delete` (expected `no matching method in call to 'delete'`, the text `get` and `post` give; the fixture pins it). Each is built against `main`'s `src` and the pull request says whether it fails there with the same text. The existing fixtures pin that `get`'s and `post`'s messages stay byte-identical.
- Verification: `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` section 3 as written. The base-wide diagnostic comparison does not apply: no `get` or `post` overload, `where` clause, adapter or call chain to `_check` changes, the new overloads are other method names (budget per name), and the message text in `_check` is checked by the expected texts. The wire trigger does not apply either: no adapter, `Request`, `Response`, `Headers` or `App.handle` change, and the slice adds no limit or status that depends on the backend (Flare answers a method with a lowercase letter with 400 before `App.handle` today, whatever routes are registered; the measured premise above). But this slice names one concrete risk the list does not cover: method handling is backend-specific (the measured premise), so a backend could reject or reshape a request with a new method before `App.handle` sees it. In `adapters/flare/test_localhost_roundtrip.mojo`, extend `test_typed_route_over_localhost_matches_test_client`: `users_app()` registers a `put`, a `patch` and a `delete` route on paths no existing route shadows, and one accepted request per method, `PUT` and `PATCH` with a body and `DELETE` without and with one, is sent with `client.send(FlareRequest(method, url, body))` with its expected status and body, and compared with `TestClient`'s new method, or, for the `DELETE` with a body, with `App.handle(Request("DELETE", target, body))`, as the existing entries are. One more entry pins the current limit: a lowercase `delete` to the `delete` route is 400 over Flare, while `App.handle(Request("delete", target))` is 404. A local `check_flare.sh` run before pushing.
- Docs: `docs/DX.md`: the new methods, their shape families, the example above, the diagnostics' method names, `TestClient`'s methods (section 10), and every statement that registration is `get` and `post` only, found by grep for `get` and `post` together and for `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` (statements that those methods are 404 stay true where no route of that method is registered); `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Current architecture" (the registration surface, its table and every statement there that names `get` or `post` as the methods, found by grep for `get`, `post`, `GET` and `POST`: the six overloads of `App.get` and `App.post`, the `Headers` slot "on `get`" (also on `delete`), the arity budget; request handling; the backend seam's `TestClient` methods; "Other current limits": methods other than `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, and bodies other than one required body on `POST` (also on `PUT` and `PATCH`)) and a revisit index row for `tests/methods_api_fail`, with the row on overloads beyond the six naming every method and linking this record (its slot-arity revisit condition); `docs/SPEC.md` (the M3 table gains `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` as shipped, and the "more HTTP methods" candidate keeps `POST` without a body, `HEAD`/`OPTIONS` and 405); `AGENT_PROGRESS.md` (the next action, and "Easy to miss": every registration method has six overloads, and `put`/`patch` share `post`'s rules, `delete` `get`'s).
- Not in the slice: `HEAD`, `OPTIONS`, other methods, a generic method entrypoint, 405 or `Allow`, bodies on `delete`, bodiless `post`, `put` or `patch`, and any change to `get`, `post` or route values.
